- **Fix:** the texture is made over the bound `MTLBuffer` with Metal's linear row alignment, which `vkGetImageSubresourceLayout` reports.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Galaxy](../../dm/games/010099C022B96000/README.md).

### Timestamp and occlusion queries

- Timestamps are their submit's completion time; occlusion queries count into a per-submit visibility buffer, copied to the pool when the submit completes.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Galaxy](../../dm/games/010099C022B96000/README.md): two timestamp pools and a 3,072-entry occlusion pool.

### Not translated in the Vulkan layer: geometry shaders

- **Symptom:** GX points and lines drawn as one-pixel points and lines.
- **Cause:** two geometry shaders (GX points and lines expanded to quads) are not translated (Metal has no geometry stage).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Galaxy](../../dm/games/010099C022B96000/README.md).

### Device switches read before the game's environment was set (Android)

- **Symptom:** a switch the Vulkan device's creation reads (`DM_VK_MESH`, `DM_VK_PLACED`, `DM_VK_ALIAS`, `DM_VK_VALIDATE`, `DM_VK_GPL` ...), set in `env.txt` or a game's Extra environment, does nothing, while the log lists it under `[nxlaunch] env`. The log has none of `vka_dev.c`'s lines (`[vk] driver:`, `[vk] device ...`).
- **Cause:** the launcher's screen gives the host its surface as soon as it has one, usually while the readiness check still holds the start back, and `nxhost_set_window` makes the device (`vka_init`): before `Native.start` set the environment and opened the log. Only `DM_VK_DRIVER` got through, set early by the readiness check. Upstream has the same order.
- **Fix:** `nxlaunch.c` keeps a window given before the start and hands it to the host in `start`, right after the environment is set (`[nxlaunch] window: given before the start, handed to the host now ...`); one given later goes straight through. The device's own lines are in the game's log again.
- **Validation:** POCO F7 Ultra, 2026-11-13: `DM_VK_ALIAS=ahb` in `env.txt` ignored before, taken after, with `[vk] DM_VK_ALIAS=ahb: placed maps are left unused` in the log.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game).

### "Not responding" at a game's start: the Vulkan device made on the launcher's UI thread (Android)

- **Symptom:** at a game's start the screen freezes for over ten seconds and Android shows its "not responding" dialog, while the game itself goes on. Reported on a Galaxy A56 (Exynos, Xclipse GPU with a RADV driver) in upstream Banana Native's issue 251; not seen on our phones.
- **Cause:** `nxhost_set_window` (vka_present.c) called `vka_init()`, and the launcher calls it on its UI thread (`Native.start`, `surfaceCreated`). On that driver `vkCreateDevice` blocks in an ioctl for over 10 s. `registerAudioDeviceCallback` on the UI thread was in the same reports' stacks (`AudioSystem.listAudioPatches`).
- **Fix:** from Banana Native `4299044a` (v0.3.23): `nxhost_set_window` makes no device. The host thread's first Vulkan call makes it (`mtl_pool_add`, `mtl_prepare`), the present thread starts with it and takes the window that came first from `g_win_new`. `GameActivity.hostSetup` registers the audio device callback on a thread of its own. The device is then also made after the game's environment is set, whoever gives the window when (the entry above; nxlaunch.c keeps its order).
- **Validation:** POCO F7 Ultra (Adreno 830), 2026-11-14: `[vk] window ...` is logged before `nxhost_main starts`, the `[vk] driver:` and `[vk] device` lines come after `guest AS` (the host thread), title at 60 fps and Kokiri Forest at 27. **Open:** no Xclipse phone here, and upstream's reporter had not confirmed the fix when this was written.
- **Found on:** upstream's issue 251 (Pokemon Pokopia); ported for [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game)'s Mali / Xclipse work.

### "Not responding" on Pause: the suspend waited for the GPU on the launcher's UI thread (Android)

- **Symptom:** the in-game panel's Pause (or Continue, or leaving the app) while the game is stalled freezes the screen, and Android shows "not responding" for the game (`am_anr ... Input dispatching timed out`); the game itself goes on once the stall ends. The ANR trace's `main` thread: `SidePanel` click → `GameActivity.pause` → `Native.lifecycle` → `nxhost_lifecycle` → `pthread_cond_wait`.
- **Cause:** `nxhost_lifecycle` (dm/host/android/input_android.c) ran the whole suspend on the caller's thread, the UI thread, under its lock: `dm_gfx_suspend(1)` waits for the submission in progress and the GPU work in flight, with no time limit. The replay thread was inside Qualcomm's compiler making a compute pipeline (32-59 s each), so the UI thread waited that long. The wait is iOS's rule (no GPU work in the background, before the app returns from going there); Android has no such rule for an activity that stops.
- **Fix:** on Android the suspend and the resume run on a lifecycle thread of their own (`dm-lifecycle`). The UI thread records the wanted state (`g_bg`, `g_pause`) under the lock and returns; the thread applies it without the lock, so later events never wait for a drain, and a Pause and a Continue that both come during one drain end in one resume. The iOS host keeps its synchronous suspend.
- **Validation:** the APK builds (2026-11-17). **Open:** a phone run that pauses during a long compile.
- **Found on:** [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Imprisonment](../../dm/games/04003F40232DE000/README.md#android) on the POCO F7 Ultra (Qualcomm 891.14): Pause pressed during its compute pipeline compiles after a Continue.

## OpenGL layer

Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition (Media.Vision's MVGL engine, SDK 6.4.0) and Balatro (LÖVE 11.4, "love-nx", with SDL and LuaJIT) render with **NVIDIA's desktop OpenGL 4.5 core profile through EGL**, not NVN. The host implements GL itself on Metal (`dm/gfx/gl/hle_gl.m`), as it does Vulkan for Hades; NVIDIA's GL driver (`subsdk0`) is not loaded. Balatro's graphics module is LÖVE's own OpenGL backend, so its choices follow what the context reports.

### Games that render with desktop OpenGL through EGL

- **Symptom:** `UNIMPL eglGetDisplay`, then `Break` right after `nv::InitializeGraphics` and `vi::OpenDefaultDisplay` (`main+0x5ed9d0`).
- **Cause:** the game renders with NVIDIA's desktop OpenGL through EGL; its NVN device (made just before) serves only a helper beside the movie player.
- **Fix:** the host's GL layer (`dm/gfx/gl/hle_gl.m`) answers the EGL imports and `eglGetProcAddress` (the rest of this section).
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md) (SDK 6.4.0, Media.Vision's MVGL engine; one boot).

### `glFlush` hands the frame so far to the GPU

- **Symptom:** the app closes with no message of its own, `[dm] CRASH sig=11 addr=0 pc=0` and a guest return address inside `main` (Well Dweller: `lr=main+0x3409a78`, the same address every time); `DM_GL_STUBS=1` then names the call: `[RED] glFlush called: not implemented, returns 0`.
- **Cause:** `glFlush` is not one of the layer's names, so `eglGetProcAddress` returns a null pointer, which the game stores in its own function table and calls when it opens a menu. `blr` into address 0 is a signal, not an abort, so nothing else says which function was wanted. A game's loader asks for the whole GL 4.6 name list (Well Dweller: 215 names, mostly 3.3+ features it never calls), so a null pointer by itself is correct; calling one is not.
- **Fix:** `glFlush` flushes pending clears and commits the open command buffer without waiting (`GLB(glFlush, …)` next to `glFinish` in `dm/gfx/gl/hle_gl.m`), and the calls after it start a new command buffer. `DM_GL_STUBS=1` remains the way to name a missing one, and `DM_GL_MISSES=1` prints every name the loader asked for and the layer does not implement.
- **Check:** Well Dweller, the same scripted input that crashed at the pause menu: the OPTIONS, GRAPHICS, STATS and PAGES screens open and the game returns to play (`[gl] frame 15300`, 35 draws per frame).
- **Found on:** [Well Dweller](../../dm/games/0100217023F6C000/README.md).

### Direct GL imports use the same implementations as the loader

- **Symptom:** `UNIMPL glCreateShader` returns 0, followed by `UNIMPL glShaderSource`, `glCompileShader`, `glGetShaderiv` and `glGetShaderInfoLog`; the game crashes in `sdk` with a null shader handle even though EGL initialized.
- **Cause:** the GL implementations were registered only in `__DATA,swgl`, which `eglGetProcAddress` searched. Normal imports search `__DATA,swhle`; a game that imports GL directly never consulted the GL table and got named unimplemented stubs instead.
- **Fix:** `GL` and `GLB` register in `swhle`, and `eglGetProcAddress` searches that same table. Both paths retain `GLB`'s big-stack entry; unsupported loader names still return null by default (`DM_GL_STUBS=1` enables diagnostic stubs). `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=010014E00DB56000 gl-imports` exercises the real import resolver and GL registrations, shader creation and source queries, wrapper addresses and missing names in both diagnostic modes, without game files.
- **Found on:** Darkest Dungeon Complete (`01008F1008DA6000`, update v327680, SDK 3.6.0, provisional profile), reported in [issue #228](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/228). The import failure is reproduced by the regression test; gameplay and run-time shader capture for this title remain unverified.

### Telling a GL game from an NVN or Vulkan one

- **Symptom:** the NSP script's provisional profile said Vulkan, and the capture boot crashed in the GL layer.
- **Cause:** `main` imports `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` for SDL's Vulkan loader, which nothing uses; the game draws with EGL and GL.
- **Fix:** `profile_guess.py` decides `graphics gl` when `main` imports `eglCreateWindowSurface` and names `glShaderSource`, and now leaves `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` out of its Vulkan count.
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md): `main` imports `nvnBootstrapLoader` and 13 EGL functions. Its graphics setup (`main+0x5ed8f0`) makes an NVN device first, which only a helper beside the movie player uses (`main+0x528000`–`+0x52c400`: a memory pool and four textures next to the movie's GL textures). Then `eglGetDisplay`, `eglBindAPI(EGL_OPENGL_API)`, a 4.5 core context, a window surface on `vi::CreateLayer(w, h)` and a glad loader (`main+0x52c460`). Of the 22 games extracted here (at the time), only Digimon matches the rule. [Balatro](../../dm/games/0100CD801CE5E000/README.md) (SDK 18.3.2, LÖVE 11.4 on OpenGL with SDL and LuaJIT; one boot): `main` imports the EGL functions and `vkGetInstanceProcAddr`, SDL's Vulkan loader, which nothing calls (`subsdk1` is NVIDIA's Vulkan driver); Hades and both Galaxies import 99 or 100 Vulkan functions, `vkCreateInstance` among them.
- **Note:** [Absolum](../../dm/games/0100A83022A22000/README.md) imports the same EGL functions (SDL3) but renders with NVN and names no GL function.

### Draws from client memory

- **Symptom:** the game draws thousands of vertices per frame and the window shows text only; a sprite scene is black while its text layer is perfect.
- **Cause:** the draw reads its vertices from a **client-memory array** — `glVertexAttribPointer` with no buffer bound, which desktop GL allows and Metal does not. The layer keeps `a->off = (uintptr_t)ptr; a->buf = S.array_buf;` in `h_glVertexAttribPointer`, so `a->buf == 0` marks client memory, and `draw_setup` used to drop such draws with one `[RED] vertex attribute 0 reads client memory or a buffer with no data (not implemented)` line. GameMaker's runner does exactly this for every sprite, background and screen composite; only its text path uses a real `GL_ARRAY_BUFFER`, which is why the text-only screens looked complete.
- **Fix:** `draw_setup` copies the vertices a draw touches into a transient `MTLBuffer` (`arena_alloc`) and binds that instead. The block keeps vertex *i* at *i* × stride from its start, as the game's array does, so the draw's first vertex, its indices and its base vertex read the same vertices unchanged; only the range *lo*..*hi* is copied: `first`..`first + count` for `glDrawArrays`, the smallest and largest index plus the base vertex for `glDrawElements` (`draw_span`, which reads the indices only when a draw has a client-memory array, so draws from vertex buffers cost nothing more), and elements 0..⌈instances / divisor⌉ for a per-instance attribute. A bound buffer with no data still drops with `[RED] vertex attribute %d reads a buffer with no data (%u)`, and a block over 128 MB is RED as well. Dropped draws are counted by reason in the layer's 300-frame line (`dropped draws since the last record: … no program, … no pass, … no attribute buffer, … no pipeline`).
- **Check:** Well Dweller's dropped-draw counter (`frame … dropped draws since the last record: … no attribute buffer …`) went from 11,885 to 0 and its draws from 274 to 22,577 per 300 frames, and the window then showed the same art as the game's own surface.
- **Found on:** [Well Dweller](../../dm/games/0100217023F6C000/README.md) (GameMaker Studio 2, SDK 22.2.0). Any GameMaker title whose screens are mostly sprites will hit it; Balatro does not, because LÖVE queues every draw with its own buffers.

### NVN in NROs under an SDL main that names GL

- **Symptom:** the NSP script took the game for an OpenGL one (`main imports EGL and names glShaderSource`), the GL shader capture found no shader, and the capture boot stopped with `[nvn-mtl] FATAL cannot read the shader map` from `h_nvnQueueSubmitCommands` called in an NRO (`nro8+0xc10d0`).
- **Cause:** Portal's `main` is SDL with its EGL video backend; it imports 20 EGL functions and names `glShaderSource` twice (SDL's GL renderer), but imports no NVN and draws nothing. The renderer is in the Source engine NROs: `materialsystem.nro` (with the D3D9 layer linked in) and `video_nx.nro` import `nvnBootstrapLoader` and draw with NVN.
- **Fix:** `profile_guess.py` `shader_sources` now reads the imports of the loose NROs in the RomFS (`romfs_nro_imports`): when one imports `nvnBootstrapLoader`, the game is an NVN game whatever `main` imports. Digimon keeps the GL rule: it has no NRO. The survey, which has only a RomFS manifest, called Portal 2 an OpenGL game and left GLSLC out of its modules until `nx_extract --manifest` also wrote the RomFS's NROs (`romfs_nro/`, 60 MB for Portal 2's 21) for it to read the same way.
- **Found on:** [Portal](../../dm/games/01007BB017812000/README.md); [Portal 2](../../dm/games/0100ABD01785C000/README.md) (the survey).

### Which GL functions a game calls

- **Symptom:** a jump to address 0 right after `glGetFloatv(0xb12)` (`lr=main+0x2d58c4`).
- **Cause:** a GL function the layer did not have (here `glVertexAttrib4fv`, LÖVE's default colours). glad asks `eglGetProcAddress` for every name it knows; the functions the game really calls come from a census of the code's loads outside the loader (`dm/tools/loader_census.py`). Every other name gets a null pointer, and a jump to address 0 names nothing.
- **Fix:** `DM_GL_STUBS=1` gives every name the layer lacks a stub of its own that reports its first call instead; `loader_census.py` lists what the code calls before the first boot.
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md): glad asks for 2,956 names and stores each in a global its code reaches through the GOT; a census of the GOT slots the code loads outside the loader gives the 79 functions the game calls: buffers, vertex arrays, textures (2D and cube, S3TC), framebuffers and renderbuffers, programs with plain `glUniform*`, `glDrawElements` / `glDrawArrays`, and three compatibility calls (`glTexEnvi`, `glColorTable`, `glPixelTransferi`). No uniform buffers, instancing or buffer mapping. [Balatro](../../dm/games/0100CD801CE5E000/README.md) (SDK 18.3.2; one boot; the missing `glVertexAttrib4fv` above): LÖVE's glad stores each pointer straight into a global of its own (no GOT slot per name), and the code reaches those globals through GOT entries that hold their addresses, which it often keeps in callee-saved registers across a loop, or picks between with `csel` (`glEnableVertexAttribArray` or `glDisableVertexAttribArray`). `loader_census.py` follows registers in a straight line for that (adrp, add, loads through relocated GOT entries, `mov`, `csel`): 2,133 names asked, about 150 called.

### GLSL source translated offline and found by hash

- Shaders are GLSL source compiled at run time with `glShaderSource` / `glCompileShader`. `gl_shaders.py` translates them offline: glslang (`-V -R`: relaxed Vulkan rules gather the loose uniforms into one `gl_DefaultUniformBlock`, whose member offsets the table lists) → SPIR-V → SPIRV-Cross. The host finds each shader by the hash of its text and its stage. Varyings are matched by name, as GL links them: `[[user(locnN)]]` becomes `[[user(<name>)]]`, so a vertex and a fragment shader translated apart still meet. `--fixup-clipspace` maps GL's clip z to Metal's.
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md): GLSL ES 3.20 source, 1,682 `.shad` files in the MVGL archives (615 distinct) and 37 strings in `main` (post effects, FXAA, shadows, the movie's YUV).

### GLSL that NVIDIA's compiler accepts and glslang does not

- Fix-ups on the preprocessed text: the GLSL ES 1.0 functions `textureCube` / `texture2D` in ES 3.20 code (with `-V` glslang reads `textureCube` as Vulkan's texture type, so a macro cannot rename it), `out vec4 gl_Position` redeclared through a macro, and `textureOffset` with an offset that is not a constant, which becomes a plain sample at P + offset ÷ level 0 size. A string in `main` says nothing of its stage, so it is translated as each stage that compiles.
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md): `textureCube` / `texture2D` in 89 fragment shaders; the redeclared `gl_Position` in the built-in sprite shader; the non-constant `textureOffset` in FXAA; one fragment shader string in `main` defines `vPosition` as `gl_Position`.

### GL shaders put together at run time and captured

- **Symptom:** in a capture run every program links with no uniforms; the game's first `Shader:send` raises a Lua error and, on the Switch, its error handler quits ([Any Lua error quits a LÖVE game](game-state.md#any-lua-error-quits-a-löve-game)).
- LÖVE builds each source in Lua from its header, its built-in code and the game's `.fs` file. The NSP script captures them on the Mac (`gl_capture.tsv`). A capture run has no library.
- **Found on:** [Balatro](../../dm/games/0100CD801CE5E000/README.md): the 35 shaders are all made before the first frame.

### The GL core profile and persistently mapped buffers

- LÖVE reads `GL_CONTEXT_PROFILE_MASK`; on a core profile it streams vertices through persistently mapped buffers (`glBufferStorage` + `glMapBufferRange` of a 3-frame ring, a `glFenceSync` per frame and `glClientWaitSync` three frames later), on a compatibility one from client memory, which Metal cannot draw from. The layer answers core: the mapping is the shared `MTLBuffer`'s memory, a fence is the serial of the command buffer being recorded, and a wait on a fence of that command buffer commits it first. The shaders then come as `#version 330 core`.
- **Found on:** [Balatro](../../dm/games/0100CD801CE5E000/README.md).

### GL uniforms, pipelines and attributes

- Uniforms are CPU copies per stage, set as bytes at a draw after they change; pipelines are cached per program by vertex layout, attachment formats, blend and colour mask; disabled vertex arrays read the attribute's current value (GL's default (0, 0, 0, 1) until `glVertexAttrib4f`) through a constant-step buffer. Without `glBindAttribLocation` the game's attribute index is the Metal one: `glGetAttribLocation` returns the shader's own attribute numbers. With it, the layer maps each Metal input to the game's index at the link.
- A GLSL `bool` is a `uint` in the SPIR-V block; `gl_shaders.py` keeps the declared type, as LÖVE's `Shader:send` takes only a boolean for a bool.
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md) (no `glBindAttribLocation`); [Balatro](../../dm/games/0100CD801CE5E000/README.md): LÖVE binds VertexPosition, VertexTexCoord, VertexColor and ConstantColor to 0–3 with `glBindAttribLocation` before each link, keeps VertexColor and ConstantColor as disabled arrays and sets their values with `glVertexAttrib4f` (the draw colour); it lists uniforms with `glGetActiveUniform` (types as GL enums, arrays as `name[0]`) and reads their initial values back with `glGetUniformfv` / `iv`.

### GL textures, swizzles and uploads

- Textures are made on first use from the levels given so far (Digimon's loader defines them level by level). An upload into a texture a pending command buffer may still read is a blit in command order; any other is a CPU write. Metal reads a channel a format lacks as 0 (alpha 1) as GL does, so only luminance / alpha formats get a swizzle: Metal refuses a swizzle on a texture that can be a render target (`Texture swizzling is not compatable with MTLTextureUsageRenderTarget`, Digimon's first story scene).
- `glTexStorage2D` makes every level up front (mipmaps filled by `glGenerateMipmap` as a blit), `glTexStorage3D` 3D and 2D array textures; the layer's units hold all four targets (96 of them, as `GL_MAX_COMBINED_TEXTURE_IMAGE_UNITS` says). A `GL_TEXTURE_SWIZZLE_*` is applied when the texture is made (a swizzled texture cannot be a render target).
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md); [Balatro](../../dm/games/0100CD801CE5E000/README.md): the game's images are mipmapped; LÖVE makes a 1x1 default texture of each type, 3D and 2D array among them; font glyphs are RG8 with a swizzle of R, R, R, G.

### GL buffers orphaned, clears as load actions

- `glBufferData` on a buffer the GPU may still read gets a new `MTLBuffer`, as GL drivers orphan it. A whole-framebuffer clear becomes the next pass's load action; a scissored or masked one is a quad.
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md).

### GL draws and framebuffers

- Batched quads share one 16-bit index buffer through `glDrawElementsBaseVertex`; canvases bind GL_FRAMEBUFFER (read and draw), check completeness, discard depth and stencil after use (`glInvalidateFramebuffer`) and clear with `glClearBufferfv`. `glBlitFramebuffer` (colour, same size) and `glReadPixels` (RGBA8) are there for multisample resolves and `Canvas:newImageData`, which the game does not use.
- **Found on:** [Balatro](../../dm/games/0100CD801CE5E000/README.md).

### GL orientation through the lower-left origin

- The layer renders as GL does, bottom row first, through the NVN layer's lower-left origin (`mtl_set_origin_lower_left`): flipped viewports, reversed winding, a flipped present. Scissor rectangles and `gl_FragCoord` need no change, and render targets read back with GL's texture coordinates.
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md).

### GL present paced by the swap interval

- Three window textures go through the NVN layer's present path (`mtl_ext_tex`, as the Vulkan swapchain), paced by the swap interval in display-link ticks.
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md): the game sets the interval to 60 ÷ its frame rate before each swap: 1 at the title and in the DigiLab, 2 from the story's opening on.

### GL movies decoded by the SDK's movie library

- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md): the USM movies' H.264 is decoded through the SDK's movie library (the host's decoder) into NV12 buffers (3,110,400 bytes a 1920x1080 frame); the game uploads the planes into a `GL_RED` and a `GL_RG` texture (two `glTexSubImage2D` a frame) and converts YUV in its own shader (in `main`, `SamplerY` / `SamplerUV`).

### Image stores into an attachment of their own pass

- **Symptom:** an image-only fragment draw's writes disappear from the subresource also bound as its color attachment when the pass ends; other image layers retain their writes.
- **Cause:** a writable image or storage alias marked every attachment written, which retained the tile store. For a loaded color attachment with no raster output or clear, that store writes the old tile contents over the shader's image writes to the same texture. Disabling the unwritten-store optimization did not avoid this correctness fault.
- **Fix (resolved in synthetic GPU tests; game validation open):** track possible shader writes separately from color-tile writes. A loaded color attachment with possible image/storage writes and no tile writes uses `DontCare` at pass end, independently of the optimization switches. Record physical color writes before resetting a merged pipeline's logical-output map; specialized fragments have conservative unknown outputs. Clear load actions and explicit clears executed in the current pass count as tile writes. A clear already landed in a standalone pass does not: marking it as a tile write retained a stale store over the following image-only draw (found while landing #808). Depth/stencil and non-loaded attachments retain their existing decisions. Mixed raster/clear and image writes to the same subresource are outside this proof and still retain the tile store.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_image_attachment_store` executes literal shaders through production draw replay and reads all four image layers on the GPU, with the unwritten-store optimization on and off. It also checks raster outputs, an earlier raster draw followed by an image-only draw, pending and explicit clears, a standalone clear before an image-only draw, unknown output reflection, a zero channel mask, a native `DontCare`-load control, and physical/logical output mapping. The standalone-clear case failed four checks before tracking the actual clear load action; all 120 checks pass with it. `t_unwritten_store` additionally preserves depth writes with a possible image alias.
- **Found on:** [DARK SOULS: REMASTERED](../../dm/games/01004AB00A260000/README.md#rendering), reported and diagnosed by Jacob-Tate in [#187](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/187#issuecomment-6519569185), with the original store fix in [34c7453](https://github.com/Jacob-Tate/nxnative/commit/34c7453e081203627752edd877dde40452afe7b2). Independently reproduced with synthetic shaders on an Apple M2 Max, macOS 26.6; no local game or iOS validation.

### Unwritten attachment stores without lookahead

- **Symptom:** read-only depth/stencil attachments are written back after every pass, and the periodic unwritten-store savings counter remains zero with the default configuration.
- **Cause:** `udc_note_draw` and `sd_finish` returned early when `DM_NVN_TBDR` was off. This also disabled the local unwritten-attachment proof, although it does not depend on future sampled-texture readers. The broader lookahead remains disabled because it does not understand bindless readers.
- **Fix:** collect per-pass write facts independently of the lookahead switch and omit only stores of attachments that were loaded and provably unchanged. Clears, depth writes, stencil writes and possible writable image/atomic/storage aliases conservatively retain stores, except for the mandatory [image-only color-attachment rule](#image-stores-into-an-attachment-of-their-own-pass). Lookahead, pass merging and cross-submission invalidation remain disabled. `DM_NVN_UNWRITTEN_DC=0` disables optional unwritten-store savings; the image-write preservation rule stays active.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_unwritten_store` performs real GPU readback against a store-all control and literal color/depth/stencil values, with lookahead disabled. It covers read-only depth/stencil, depth writes, stencil writes, pending clears, and writable images (including native image atomics) and both storage-buffer binding forms. `t_unwritten_store --bench` runs a synthetic 32-pass 1080p workload with balanced on/off order; it is not a game FPS measurement.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata US 1.0.3](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md#factory-combat-capture-and-9s-texture-follow-up), during iPhone factory-combat performance investigation. The change passes the GPU proof and rendering regressions on Mac; iPhone gameplay benefit remains to be measured.

### Texture content from storage on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) menus with their panels but no text; textures that read zeros; a level whose auto exposure blows every light into a white bloom blob.
- **Cause:** the Vulkan replay keeps each texture in an image of its own, not over the pool, and filled an image from guest memory only when it was compressed or marked CPU-written. On the Switch a texture is its storage: uncompressed textures with a packaged layout (BNTX assets the game loads into its pools, the font sheets among them) read zeros. The other way round, a linear render target the GPU draws into is pool memory on the Switch (and on Metal, which makes linear textures on the pool buffer), and the CPU reads it there: agl renders its luminance into small linear R16F targets (1x1, 320x180) and reads them back for the eye adaptation.
- **Fix:** `storage_mode` in `vka_tex.c` follows `nvn_mtl.m` (`tex_from_storage`, `tex_cpu_content`, `tex_init_fill`, `tex_linear`): packaged layout, CPU-written, linear and block-compressed-with-data textures are uploaded and tracked with the same dirty-page tracker (`nvn_dirty.c`: write-protected after each upload, re-uploaded at the next submission after a CPU write); CPU_NO_ACCESS / CPU_UNCACHED / flushed storage fills once; anything the GPU writes stops tracking. Linear textures the GPU wrote are copied back into their storage at the end of the submission (`vka_tex_writeback`). `DM_VK_TEX_TRACK=0` uploads once without write protection.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49: 1,512 storage uploads by the title, about 5,000 write-protection faults in the first level, the glyph cache (1024x8192 R8, linear) re-uploaded as the CPU rasterizes into it.

### Textures bound as shader images without the image flag on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) the whole picture one flat purple (lilac) colour in Donkey Kong Bananza's opening area, on the system driver and on Turnip, with frame generation on or off; `[RED] vk: shader 5e0110401b4a3d8f binding 2: texture ID 1663 has no view of shape 0x1 (storage): it reads as unbound (… format 0x3f 1536x864x1 levels 1 flags 0; … usage 0x17)`. A save past the opening (the test seed, in the first level) never shows it.
- **Cause:** the replay gave an image storage use only when its texture had NVN's `IMAGE` flag (`img_create`, `vka_tex.c`). The game makes the 1,536×864 R11G11B10 target with flags 0, renders into it, then binds it in a compute shader's image slot; Metal's host treats that binding as image intent ([Image bindings supply shader-write intent](nvn-objects.md#image-bindings-supply-shader-write-intent)), the Vulkan replay bound nothing, so the compute pass's writes went nowhere and the passes after it composed the frame from an image that never held the scene.
- **Fix:** a storage binding (and an image registration) is image intent: an image made from then on takes storage use, and one already made without it is remade with it on the spot (`img_storage_upgrade`, `vka_tex.c`): a new image with the same shape plus storage, every level copied, swapped into the same `vka_img` so every serial and view sharing it follows, the old image destroyed once the submissions that may use it complete, the pool slots naming it rewritten. A draw ends its render pass first (`make_images` asks `vka_tex_id_storage_pending`). An image a thread outside recording holds (the present, frame generation, capture: `holds`) is not remade then; that binding stays unbound and the next one tries again. `DM_VK_IMG_UPGRADE=0` (live) brings back the old behaviour, for A/B. The log says `bound as a shader image without the image flag, remade with storage use` once per image (16 at most).
- **Check:** PNM-N49, 2026-11-02, a save holding only `config.sav` (the opening), the same APK: `DM_VK_IMG_UPGRADE=0` purple with the 8 red lines; on: one remake (serial 4471, about 1,950 pool slots rewritten), no red line, the scene drawn, on the system driver with frame generation off and on, and on Turnip v32 with it on.
- **Resolved:** the opening's picture was then smeared in blocks of about 8 pixels, and the level's 3D picture pixelated: with the remake the upscaler network's output reached its image, and the network computed wrong tiles in 64-lane subgroups ([Warp gathers of the Ampere tensor instructions in a 64-lane subgroup](shaders.md#warp-gathers-of-the-ampere-tensor-instructions-in-a-64-lane-subgroup)).
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49 (the play app on a new game).

### Present waiting for a fence still being recorded on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) the GPU stops while a level loads: `[RED] vk: GPU: fence N+2 not reached after 2.0 s (at N)` over and over, `giving back pool memory …: the GPU was not done with it after 2 s: freed anyway`, then the phone's system server restarts (a soft reboot). Now: `[RED] vk: GPU hang: …` and the host exits; `[vk] present ticket … came while fence … was still being recorded` shows each time the race came up.
- **Cause:** `mtl_present` took `vka_fence_last()`, the last fence *reserved*. `mtl_sub_commit` reserves its fence at its top and hands the batch to the queue at its end, and `nvnQueuePresentTexture` does not take the submit lock, so a present during another thread's commit queued its copy with a wait for a timeline value only a batch queued after it signals: a wait-before-signal on one queue, which the Adreno 840 driver never resolves. The present thread's priority decides how often the race comes up: once in a first-level load at normal priority, 128 times with it at display priority (android-perf's ADPF session), which is why that branch hung and `android-int` did not. Freeing a pool after the 2 s wait then freed memory under queued work.
- **Fix:** the present waits for `vka_fence_queued()`, the last value a `vkQueueSubmit` signals (kept under the queue lock), and `present_one` clamps to it under that lock. Hangs no longer escalate (`vka_dev.c`): a fence without progress for `DM_VK_HANG_S` (6 s) is a declared hang: a `[RED]` line with what the stuck submission held, nothing more submitted or presented, every fence done, pool memory the GPU may still use kept instead of freed, and `dm_host_shutdown` (`DM_VK_HANG_EXIT=0` runs on without the GPU). `DM_VK_TEST_HANG=1,<ticket>` / `2,<ticket>` makes one present wait for an unqueued / never-signalled value, to check both.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49 (android-perf c1ff15fa, three hangs loading the first level).

### Placed pool memory split by mprotect on the Adreno driver

- **Symptom:** (Android) objects with black cores and white bloom (NaN in the float targets), magenta and wrong colours that change from run to run; `DM_VK_TEX_TRACK=0` makes them go away. `DM_VK_NANSCAN`'s storage watch logs texture storage that changes with its pages read-only and no CPU fault, and the CPU and a GPU copy of the same bytes disagree.
- **Cause:** the Adreno 840's kernel driver (kgsl) fills a page of a placed Vulkan map on its first touch, and in a map that mprotect has split it takes the page at the touch's distance from the start of that piece, not from the start of the allocation. The dirty tracker write-protects texture pages after each upload (`nvn_dirty.c`), which splits the pool's map; every later first touch of a page past a split then read and wrote the allocation's first pages. The game's writes went to the wrong memory, the GPU read zeros where the CPU had written, and the CPU read other textures' bytes, so textures were uploaded with blocks of other textures (ASTC 5x4 storage holding 8x8 blocks, which the Adreno decodes to NaN). A page already in the page table keeps its page through any split.
- **Fix:** `place()` in `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_mem.c` faults every page of a new placed map in (`MADV_POPULATE_READ`, else a touch per page) before anything can split it. The driver commits its memory at allocation, so this costs no memory: 1.65 GB in 112 ms on the phone. `DM_VK_NANSCAN` runs a placed memory test once (`ptest` in `vka_tex.c`: pages written, write-protected and back, compared between the CPU and a GPU copy): before the fix four of six pages read another page's bytes, after it none.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49: NaN texels in the tunnel's 2304x1296 R11G11B10F target went from 9,000-470,000 a frame to 2; the bloom blobs on lamps, rocks and the mine sign and the magenta are gone.

### Write protections that failed left CPU-written textures stale on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) a texture the CPU writes after its first use keeps its first content (text missing, an old picture) with no re-upload and no write-protection fault for it. Now: `[RED] dirty-page tracking: mprotect(read-only) of … failed (errno …) … process mappings N, vm.max_map_count M`, once a pool; `mprotect failures N (the last: …)` on the `packaged textures` line; `pages whose protection failed` in the dirty-tracking state that `DM_VK_NANSCAN` and the linear-texture lines print.
- **Cause:** `nvn_dirty_protect` (`dm/gfx/nvn/nvn_dirty.c`) counted a failed `mprotect` and marked the pages protected anyway. A page left writable never faults, so no CPU write into it was seen and the textures over it kept their first upload. Unprotecting had the opposite defect: a failed `mprotect(read-write)` marked a read-only page writable, and a write to it was re-executed from the fault handler forever. The counter was never printed, so whether the phone's `mprotect` fails is not measured yet. A likely failure is `ENOMEM` past `vm.max_map_count`: every protection splits a mapping (see [Memory maps run out under Turnip during a compute-heavy video](memory.md#memory-maps-run-out-under-turnip-during-a-compute-heavy-video-android)).
- **Fix:** a protection that fails makes its pages writable again, keeps them unprotected and gives them the highest generation. Making them writable again can fail as well (Linux applies a protection mapping by mapping, so a read-only one can have landed on part of the run): then each page is retried alone, and a page that still fails is kept protected with the highest generation, never marked writable, so its first write faults into the unprotect path (which makes it writable and stamps it, or reports it) instead of being re-executed forever. Every texture over them then counts as written at each check, and the clock moves so that the check runs: they are uploaded again at every submission, like untracked storage, until a later protection of those pages succeeds (that stamps them: one more upload, then normal tracking). An unprotection that fails leaves the page protected and does not claim the fault, so the crash handler reports it. The first failure in a pool logs a `[RED]` line with the errno, the range, the number of the process's mappings and `vm.max_map_count`. Also: partial re-uploads of linear textures (`upload_rows_tracked` in `vka_tex.c`) now count in "re-uploads on pool content change". Before, they were left out of that count, which read as "never re-uploaded". `DM_VK_LINLOG` (live, default 64 lines; 0 = none) logs each linear texture once at its first sample (its content mode, whether its storage is placed memory, its tracked range and page states), once at its first re-upload, and once at its first GPU write (the recording site: from then on its storage is no longer followed).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (POCO F7 Ultra, 2026-11-07), while looking for why its text boxes come out empty. Not that cause: on the phone no protection failed (`mprotect failures 0`), and its line textures held their glyphs when first uploaded.

### GPU end held back by recording and a clocked-down GPU on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) a game whose frame-rate controller measures frame start to GPU end stays at a longer present interval (20 fps) though its GPU work fits the shorter one: the frame statistics show 20-25 ms of real GPU time (`DM_VK_GPUPROF`) yet the game blocks 35-45 ms a frame in SyncWait (`@sync_ms`), and its submission completes 20-33 ms after the replay finished recording it (`DM_VK_SYNCLOG`).
- **Cause:** two things after the recording. The parts a submission goes to the queue in (`DM_VK_CHUNK`) were cut only between passes, so a pass of 1,700 draws reached the GPU only once all of it was recorded and its 14-18 ms of GPU work came after the recording. And the GPU sat idle while the start of each frame was recorded, so its governor clocked it down: the same replayed frame took 21-28 ms of GPU time with the clock up and 93-99 ms after two seconds of idle GPU. The 252 full barriers a frame were not the cost: every step that is not a pass came to 0.6-1.1 ms a frame.
- **Fix:** `DM_VK_CHUNK_PASS` (default 200 since 2026-11-22, 600 before; `vka_exec.c`) also ends a pass that has had that many draws in the part and submits the part; the next draw opens the pass again over the same targets with loadOp LOAD after the pass end's barrier, so contents and order are kept (the splits cost no measurable GPU time). `DM_VK_GPU_FLOOR` (default 100, `vka_dev.c`) sets the queue's GPU clock floor with `VK_QCOM_queue_perf_hint` (100: the highest clock, 0: the driver's governor). The GPU then finishes 6-10 ms after the recording and SyncWait drops to 0-3 ms a frame; what is left of the measure is the recording itself. `DM_VK_GPUPROF=n` times every step between full barriers in one frame of n (GPU time by kind, the costliest steps with their barrier site, draws and attachments); ADPF reports carry the frame's GPU time (`DM_VK_ADPF_GPU`), with no effect seen on this phone. The floor costs power while the GPU works; `DM_VK_GPU_FLOOR_AUTO=1` (live) follows the frame's span (it stayed at 100 in the level) and `DM_VK_GPU_FLOOR_AUTO=2` raises it only from a submission's commit to its completion, leaving the recording to the driver's governor (2026-11-03: the count of pending submissions is taken when the submission posts its span and given back by that post, and the floor is decided under the queue lock; before, any completed submission with draws took one off and a completion's 0 could land after the next commit's floor). Mode 2 has not been measured on the phone (`android/STUTTER-TEST.md` section 5).
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49 (Adreno 840): with the floor at 100 and the splits its controller chose interval 2 (30 fps) for 70-85 % of the frames at a CPU cap of 2.23 GHz (frame 36 ms on average instead of 50), none with the floor at 70 or the driver's governor in the same session, and none at a cap of 2.0 GHz (recording 33-36 ms a frame). [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on the POCO F7 Ultra (Adreno 830), with `DM_VK_CHUNK` and `DM_VK_CHUNK_PASS` at 200 (now the defaults) against 600, two runs each (2026-11-22). Hyrule Field with its grass, 891.14: 22.5 to 25.2 fps, frame p50 44 to 39 ms, GPU 37 to 35 ms. Kokiri Forest, Turnip V39: 19.4 to 20.7 fps, GPU unchanged. Hyrule Field without its grass, V39: 29.0 to 29.9 fps.

### Recording held back by the driver's command recording on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) the replay thread's recording time (`recording … ms a frame` in the replay statistics) decides the frame rate: Donkey Kong Bananza's level recorded ~29-31 ms a frame (~3.8 us a draw, ~8,000 draws) and its frame-rate controller (frame start to GPU end) fell from 30 to 20 fps whenever the phone's CPU cap dropped to 2.0 GHz.
- **Cause:** about a third of the replay thread's time was inside the Adreno driver recording the commands (simpleperf with call graphs: `vkCmdPushDescriptorSet` 19 %, `vkCmdDrawIndexed` 9 %, `vkCmdBeginRendering` 4 %, `vkResetCommandBuffer` 4 %), one call after another with the replay's own work on one thread.
- **Fix:** the command encoder (`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_enc.c`, `DM_VK_ENC`, live, default 1): the vk table's `vkCmd*` entries copy each call into the submission's command buffer (arguments and the arrays they point to) into a ring, and a second thread (`vk-enc`) makes them in order. A part's end, submit and the next part's reset and begin (`DM_VK_CHUNK`, `DM_VK_CHUNK_PASS`) go through the same ring, so the replay thread waits only at the submission's commit (`vka_enc_stop`). Calls from other threads or into other command buffers, and a call with a `pNext` chain the encoder does not copy, go to the driver directly (the latter after the ring is drained). Both threads wait for each other on the ring's head and tail with WFE, then a futex. The replay statistics log the encoder's calls, busy time and the replay thread's waits a frame.
- **Then:** (2026-11-03, built, not phone-checked) the replay thread binds only the vertex buffers that changed since the last draw in the command buffer, consecutive slots in one call (`DM_VK_VB_FILTER`, live, default 1; profile p2: `vin_bind` 13 % of the replay thread), reads a run of deferred texture handles in one `process_vm_readv` (3.4 %), binds a driver segment (c0, c2, the push bank: 2.5 KB filled into fresh scratch per segment per draw, ~60-80 MB of writes a frame, which also made every draw's push descriptor set unique) again when its bytes equal the last draw's in the submission, the same for the raw global memory table while the placed segments stand and for the alpha test's support copy (`DM_VK_DRV_MEMO`, live, default 1; graphics stages only: an indirect dispatch's counts are GPU-copied into its c0), sets the vertex input state only when it changes, keeps the recording thread's address lookup cache outside TLS (`DM_VK_LC_GLOBAL`, at start; the dynamic TLS resolver was in the 8 % the lookup took), binds the index buffer only when it changes, can push only the descriptors that changed since the last push of the same layout (`DM_VK_PUSH_DELTA`, default 0 until checked on the driver: Vulkan allows incremental push descriptor updates), and hands its calls to the encoder in batches of `DM_VK_ENC_BATCH` bytes (default 4096; 0 = each call) and at every wait, sync, part switch and stop, so a part's submit is never held back (the encoder spent 70 % of its cycles in its own loop, woken by each of ~45,000 head stores a frame). Review fixes (same day): the segment memo has one slot each for c0, c2 and the push bank only (any other driver bank shared the push bank's slot, so an all-zero 0x100-byte push block could be bound with a 0xA00-byte range); the vertex input state is also set again after every pipeline change (Vulkan keeps it across binds, but drivers have lost dynamic vertex input there).
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49 (Adreno 840), same session, CPU cap 2.5 then 2.23 GHz: recording 29.1 → 18.1-19.5 ms a frame, the encoder busy ~14.5 ms, frame 34.6 → 33.3 ms on average (every frame at 30 fps, the game's SyncWait 27 → 0 ms). `nxreplay` (inline, `DM_VK_WORKER=0`): 41-45 → 29-31 ms.

### Game barriers ending the draw pass on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) more render passes a frame than the game's render target changes explain (the replay statistics' `render passes` and `full barriers`; DKB's level ~100 passes and ~265 full barriers a frame); on a tiler each pass end stores every attachment and the reopening pass loads it again.
- **Cause:** every game barrier (`nvnCommandBufferBarrier`, `OP_BARRIER`) ended the open pass and recorded a full barrier (`vka_exec.c`), so a barrier between draws into the same targets split the pass in two. Inside one pass Vulkan already keeps the attachment writes of its draws in rasterization order; what such a barrier can still order is memory the earlier draws wrote with shader stores, and an attachment of the pass that a later draw samples. `nvn_mtl.m` keeps its render encoder across a barrier unless the pass has shader writes.
- **Fix:** `vka_rp_game_barrier` (`vka_draw.c`) with `DM_VK_BAR_KEEP` (live, **default 1 for every game since 2026-11-13**, was 0; `0` brings back a pass end at every barrier) takes a barrier inside the pass when no draw of the pass wrote memory; the draws after it are checked (`bar_hazard`): one that writes memory (a storage buffer or image, raw global stores, a geometry shader's claim), reads the texture pool directly, has bindings the walk could not read, or samples a texture whose image is an attachment of the pass (any level), may share memory with one, or is not mapped (`vka_tex_id_in_pass` with `in_fb_or_alias`, `vka_tex.c`, after Erick's `b6a3bfb`: sparse- or virtual-pool-bound images can bind the same pages as a target, and textures of other serials can lie over a target's guest storage; ranges are compared half-open and unknown storage counts as an overlap; since the 2026-11-13 review two residency images (`vbind`, tiles bound to the physical pages behind their mappings) also count as an overlap at any guest addresses, which Erick's check missed) ends the pass first. Why it holds for every game: Vulkan keeps a pass's attachment writes in rasterization order, every op that records GPU work outside the pass ends it first (dispatches, copies, clears of other targets, inline updates not kept), and the sync records it stays open across record no memory access the barrier would order, so the kept barrier is put off to the pass end (`vka_rp_end`'s barrier, scoped or full, "Pass-end barriers"), never dropped; Banana Native ships it on since v0.2.0 (eden/v0.3.11 `vka_draw.c`), which is evidence, not proof. The replay statistics line `game barriers inside a draw pass per frame: …` counts the barriers that came with a pass open, those taken inside it and those that ended it later, split by why (writing memory, reading the pool directly, bindings not walked, sampling a target or what may share its memory), with the switch on or off; if the pool reason dominates, narrowing it (Erick's `da81e0d` / `483eba9`, not ported) is the next step. `DM_VK_PUSH_DELTA` (partial push descriptor updates, `vka_draw.c` `push_same`) stays at 0 until the same phone check; its comment now states the Vulkan rule it relies on and the sites that forget the last push.
- **Check:** the Gradle APK build and `dm/tools/tests/test_vk_bar_alias.py` (the overlap truth table and the source structure); open: the phone A/B (the same scene, 1200 frames after warm-up, `DM_VK_BAR_KEEP=0` then `1` through `env-<id>.txt`: `GPU busy` p50, the passes a frame and the split above; the picture: post-processing, reflections, shadows, anything that samples a render target after a barrier). If an artefact appears that the split cannot explain, the default goes back to 0.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) (from the code; not yet measured on the phone: the count says how many of the level's passes the switch can merge).

### Texture pool slots written again unchanged on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) none in the picture: replay CPU time in `vkUpdateDescriptorSets` for texture pool slots (descriptor set 1, bindings 0 and 2) that already hold the view being written. Every registration dirties its texture ID, and a pool read, a remade image or the pool reads' blind fallback dirty many at once.
- **Cause:** `vka_tex_prepare` (`vka_tex.c`) sent every dirty slot's write, also when the slot held that view already. Besides the driver's cost, the set is `UPDATE_AFTER_BIND` with `UPDATE_UNUSED_WHILE_PENDING`, which allows updating only descriptors that pending command buffers do not use dynamically, the same value or not.
- **Fix:** general, after Erick's `6e73a21` with another key: a per-ID memo (`g_pool_written`, sampled and storage slots apart, grown with the registry) of the view each slot holds and the generation of its handle's bucket (`g_vgen_h`, 4096 buckets), which `img_destroy` bumps for each view it destroys, after the global `g_vgen` and before `vkDestroyImageView`. A slot whose write names the same view with its bucket unchanged is skipped: a binding's descriptor type is fixed and its layout always GENERAL, and a view handle can be recycled only after a `vkDestroyImageView`. Erick keyed it on `g_tgen`, which moves at every unref and left his memo nearly always cold (his build showed no fps change); one global destroy counter would do the same in a streaming game, where images retire most frames. `write_tex` lets the texture lock go, and a concurrent `img_destroy` from `vka_after` may destroy the view it read: `g_vgen` is read before it and again after the bucket, and a slot is skipped or remembered only when no destroy began in between (otherwise it is written and its memo dropped; before the 2026-11-13 review one read after `write_tex` could remember a dead view with its bumped generation, and skip its recycled handle later). The memo's pointer is read after `write_tex` too. The null view is deduplicated the same way; null to a view and a view to null are writes. It relies on `vka_tex_prepare` being the only writer of those bindings and `img_destroy` the only place pool views are destroyed (both stated in the code). `DM_VK_POOL_SLOT_DEDUP=0` (live, default 1) writes every dirty slot, as before; the memo is kept with it off, so turning it on live is safe. The log adds `pool slot writes: N written, M skipped as unchanged` every 600 prepares.
- **Check:** the Gradle APK build and `dm/tools/tests/test_vk_pool_slot_dedup.py` (a model of the memo under random view creation, destruction inside and outside `write_tex` and handle recycling, the review's dead-view case, a single generation read shown to fail, and the source structure); open: the phone measurement (`DM_VK_POOL_SLOT_DEDUP=0` then `1`: the counts, replay CPU time, no wrong textures or device loss across scene loads, the prune and RAM pressure). Expected gain small (CPU only).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (from Erick's fork and the code; open: the phone A/B).

### Where the GPU stopped when the device is lost (Android, open)

- **Symptom:** (Android) the run is lost minutes into play, long after the first frames: `[RED] vk: GPU hang: …`, or a submission that returns -4 (the device is lost). A driver without `VK_EXT_device_fault` says nothing about which command faulted, and after a -4 nothing more can be read from the device.
- **Cause:** open. Upstream Banana Native collects such losses on Ocarina of Time, Donkey Kong Bananza and Mario Kart World under one umbrella (Forgejo #409; its entry "A GPU hang or lost device in warm play" in [its rendering reference](https://git.eden-emu.dev/moceure/BananaNative/src/tag/eden/v0.3.50/docs/reference/rendering.md)); these switches are to find where the GPU stopped.
- **Diagnostics (ported from Banana Native v0.3.50; not a fix):** GPU breadcrumbs, `DM_VK_CRUMBS` (restart, default 0 = off; `vka_exec.c` "GPU breadcrumbs", `vka_crumb`): at `1` the GPU writes the next number into a host-visible ring at each part's start and end of a submission, at `2` also before each dispatch (its shader key, `vka_draw.c`) and after each render pass (its size). When a hang is declared the ring is read straight from memory, without a driver call, and the hang report prints `GPU hang: breadcrumbs (DM_VK_CRUMBS=…): the GPU wrote up to … of … recorded` with what was recorded after the last number written: the fault lies there. Level 2 adds two barriers a dispatch and a pass, so timing changes. `DM_VK_PART_SERIAL=1` (live, slow) idles the queue before each submission is recorded, so the GPU never runs while the replay thread records: a loss that goes away with it is a race between the host's writes while recording (descriptor tables, scratch, uploads, placement) and the GPU's reads; one that stays lies in the commands. Upstream's trace of its gates' openings is not ported: this tree has no gates.
- **Found on:** upstream's reports of [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md), [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) and [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md); here no run with the switches yet (open).

### Zero-grid dispatches and zero-count draws on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) none in the picture: GPU time and replay work for dispatches and draws that do nothing. In `DM_VK_GPUPROF` and `DM_VK_DISPATCH_LOG` Ocarina's frames have about three direct dispatches a frame at groups `0,0,0` (`cee08c63...`, `510c344f...`, `e855420a...`), each recorded with its descriptors, its pipeline bind and the full barrier after it.
- **Cause:** `vka_dispatch` (`vka_draw.c`) guarded only the `vkCmdDispatch` call: a direct grid with a zero or negative count still walked and pushed its descriptors, bound its pipeline and recorded the full barrier every dispatch ends with (a GPU drain on a tiler). `vka_draw` had the same for a direct draw of count 0 on the plain path (only the native and emulated mesh paths returned early).
- **Fix:** a direct dispatch with no workgroups returns right after the pass ends and its `DM_VK_DISPATCH_LOG` index is taken (the index still counts every dispatch; the log line says `no workgroups, nothing recorded`), and a direct draw of count 0 returns before its pipeline, descriptors and pass. Both run no invocations in Vulkan or NVN, and NVN orders nothing by them, so the stream is the guest's without them; the barrier dropped only ordered work that ends with its own barrier. Indirect and indirect-count work (the GPU's counts), the emulated mesh path's own recursive draw (`g_mesh_pull`), frames `DM_VK_GPUPROF` times and, for dispatches, `DM_VK_NANSCAN` keep the full path. General, no profile key (after Erick's Ocarina fork, commit `23dcc73`, without its game gate; the zero-count draw is ours). `DM_VK_ZERO_DISPATCH=0` / `DM_VK_ZERO_DRAW=0` (live, default 1) record the old path for an A/B; `dm/tools/tests/test_vka_noop_skips.py` checks the structure. Expected gain small (under 0.2 ms a frame on Ocarina; Erick saw no fps change). A plain or indexed zero-count draw still takes its `DM_VK_DRAW_LOG` index (`nothing recorded`), so indices match with the switch off. What the A/B should watch: occlusion results (a zero-count draw that hit the full query ring or a `SKIP` used to add one lost sample, pushing a report towards visible; it now adds none, which is correct but can change a game's culling), command-buffer chunk boundaries (no pass opens for the draw), and the stats overlay (a zero-grid dispatch that the old path would have skipped for a missing library or pipeline now counts as a dispatch). For an emulated mesh draw of count 0, `DM_VK_MESH_DIAG_LIMIT`'s ordinals no longer count it and `DM_VK_MESH_BATCH_FIT=0` no longer brings back the old count-0 path (only `DM_VK_ZERO_DRAW=0` does); `DM_VK_TEXSTAT_AT` and `DM_VK_DISPATCH_SKIP_KEY` never see a zero-grid dispatch. Checked: every producer the dropped dispatch barrier could have ordered ends with its own barrier (`vka_rp_end`, `pc_flush`, the `vka_exec.c` clears, copies and UB updates, the texture uploads and retype copies in `vka_tex.c`); open: the phone A/B.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (from Erick's measurement and the code; open: the phone A/B of both switches and a Mario Kart World race start).

### A full barrier after every dispatch on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) GPU time that grows with a game's compute dispatches a frame while its draws stay the same: the `[vk] pace up to` lines' `GPU busy` and the `replay up to frame` lines' `full barriers` per frame climb with the dispatches (one full barrier of the host's per dispatch, on top of the game's own).
- **Cause:** NVN orders a dispatch against the work after it only through the game's barriers (`nvnCommandBufferBarrier`, `OP_BARRIER`), which the replay records itself. `vka_dispatch` (`vka_draw.c`) also recorded a full barrier (`ALL_COMMANDS` to `ALL_COMMANDS`, every write to every read) after every dispatch: consecutive dispatches never overlapped, and a game with its own barrier after most dispatches paid two each. On a tiler each full barrier drains the GPU and flushes its caches.
- **Fix:** general (`vka_draw.c` "Dispatch order", `vka_disp_settle`; ported from Banana Native eden/v0.2.9 `ad509ad8`, unchanged at eden/v0.3.11, not from Erick's Ocarina-only opt-in `DM_VK_OOT_DISPATCH_OVERLAP`): the barrier after a dispatch is left pending, keyed by its command buffer, and recorded only before other GPU work in it: draws (before a part of the submission ends at one), clears, copies, buffer fills, inline uniform updates and counter reports (`vka_exec.c` `exec`), an indirect dispatch's copy of its group counts and `DM_VK_TEXSTAT_AT`'s copies (`vka_draw.c`), a part's end (`chunk`) and the submission's end (`sub_commit`). A full barrier recorded meanwhile, the game's included, settles it (`vka_tex.c` `vka_barrier_at`); `vka_img_barrier` and the end's HOST-stage barrier do not. The host steps that write into the command buffer in the middle of a dispatch's set-up (an image's first use or memory of its own, a storage upgrade, uploads) begin with `vka_img_barrier` on their image or with a full barrier, so they stay ordered after the earlier dispatches. Kept at once: a dispatch whose shared memory is in the spill buffer (the next one reuses its slices), and under `DM_VK_NANSCAN`. `DM_VK_DISPATCH_ORDER=1` (live, default 0) records it after every dispatch again, for an A/B in one session. The replay statistics add `dispatches per frame: D; barriers after them recorded S, not needed N (DM_VK_DISPATCH_ORDER)`. `dm/tools/tests/test_vk_dispatch_order.py` checks the settle points in the source.
- **Check:** built for Android (the Gradle APK build) and the source test; open: the phone measurement (the `full barriers` per frame should fall by about the dispatches a frame, `GPU busy` p50 with `DM_VK_DISPATCH_ORDER=1` then `0` in one session, and no flicker in compute-driven effects), on this tree's games (Mario Kart World, Zelda: Ocarina of Time) as well as Pokopia: the lever relies on each game's own barriers between dependent dispatches, which upstream checked only on Pokopia, and nothing turns it off by itself when a game lacks one (`scripts/regress.py` runs the Mac host and does not reach `dm/gfx/vk_android`). The byte-for-byte old command stream needs both switches off (`DM_VK_DISPATCH_ORDER=1` and `DM_VK_BAR_NARROW=0`): with only the first, a game barrier right after the host's full barrier is merged into it and records nothing (the same order). A dispatch whose raw global memory write log falls back to scratch (`gmem_log`, all 256 logs in flight) gets a log of its own, so overlapping dispatches never share one. With the lever on, `DM_VK_GPUPROF` profiles are not comparable with older ones: the pending and narrowed barriers do not pass through `vka_barrier_at` (no `vka_gp_mark`) and the GPU no longer drains between dispatches; a `TIMESTAMP_TOP` counter report after a game barrier that `vka_gbar.c` took is no longer placed after the earlier dispatches (reported GPU times shift, the picture does not).
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md) upstream (Banana Native: about 1,000 dispatches a frame after an hour, GPU time 5 → 22 ms on an Adreno 750 with Turnip); not yet measured on this tree's games.

### Inline uniform updates on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) passes and full barriers a frame that follow a game's inline uniform updates (`nvnCommandBufferUpdateUniformBuffer`, `OP_UPDATE_UB`): the `replay up to frame` lines of Ocarina of Time's logs (on disk, 2026-11) show about 80 updates, 165 render passes and 700 full barriers a frame for about 2,290 draws; Mario Kart World and Pokopia make 0-1 updates a frame.
- **Cause:** `vka_exec.c` recorded each update as one `vkCmdCopyBuffer` per placed run followed by a full ALL_COMMANDS barrier, and an update inside an open pass went through the first switch's default case, which ends the pass (a store and a reload of its attachments on a tiler) so the copy can be recorded outside it. The Metal host has deferred them since upstream's `DM_NVN_UB_DEFER` ([Inline uniform updates inside a render pass](#inline-uniform-updates-inside-a-render-pass)).
- **Fix:** general, three steps with a live switch each (`vka_exec.c` "Inline uniform updates"; after Erick's Ocarina fork `0220a33` to `36c9eba`, without its game gate): (1) the barrier after the copies is TRANSFER_WRITE to ALL_COMMANDS with MEMORY_READ / MEMORY_WRITE (`vka_transfer_write_barrier`, `DM_VK_UB_SCOPED`, default 1): what came before ended with a full barrier already, so the copies' own writes are all it publishes; (2) consecutive disjoint updates, also across state records and the lists' own flow (BEGIN, JUMP, CALL, END back into a calling list), are staged into one `vkCmdCopyBuffer` and one barrier (`DM_VK_UB_BATCH`, default 1), flushed before any other record (before a part can end at a draw), on an overlapping destination or another buffer pair (the last write wins), with safety flushes at a part's and the submission's end (`DM_VK_UB_BATCH=0` records a barrier after every update, also one that staged nothing: with `DM_VK_UB_SCOPED=0` too, the old recording); (3) an update inside an open pass is kept to the pass end (`DM_VK_UB_DEFER`, **default 0** until the phone A/B): its bytes copied into scratch at once, its copy recorded by `vka_rp_end` after the pass's full barrier (Erick's version copied before it: a write-after-read race against draws that read the range in place; no second barrier follows, Erick's `8538623` by Vulkan's dependency rules), and the pass's later draws bind a shadow of the range (`vka_tex_gw_shadow_read`, the CPU's bytes with the GPU writes not done yet, then the kept updates), made by `ub_hazard` before the pass is used: a shadow it cannot make (no scratch, a write it cannot read, more new ones than the 32-entry memo) ends the pass instead, so no draw binds a range without its kept update. The descriptor walk's CPU reads (`cpu_read`: texture handles kept in a uniform buffer, a uniform copy) have the kept updates over them too, so a draw never mixes the new constants with the handles before them (`vka_ub_cpu_overlay`, `DM_VK_UB_CPU_OVERLAY`, live, default 1; it acts only while updates are kept). Not kept: after a draw of the pass wrote memory, after a game barrier taken in it, with clears waiting, over a target's storage, over 64 KB, past 128. A draw that could read a kept range otherwise ends the pass before its images and the pass (`vka_draw.c` `ub_hazard`): pool reads, raw global memory, buffer textures, a texture over the range, a storage buffer over it, a uniform range the shadow cannot read or that a writable storage buffer of the draw overlaps, vertex buffers, and the exact index, indirect and indirect-count ranges (`DM_VK_UB_DEFER_IDX=0`: any indexed or indirect draw, Erick's rule); a signalled event ends the pass too. The replay statistics add `inline uniform updates per frame: ...` (staged regions and copies, kept, shadows, flushed, passes ended by a hazard or a boundary, reasons not kept) and `transfer-write barriers` on the `replay up to frame` line. `dm/tools/tests/test_vk_ub_inline.py` checks the structure in the source; the range arithmetic and the batch and kept-list rules are `vka_ubq.h`, which `t_android_ubq` (`dm/host/tests`) runs against random update sequences applied one by one (the last write wins) and a draw's index and indirect-count ranges.
- **Check:** the Gradle APK build, the source test and `t_android_ubq`; `scripts/regress.py` (macOS only) not run; open: the phone measurement (configurations: all three off, then each step on; Ocarina's full barriers should fall by about its 80 updates a frame with the batch, its passes by the updates kept with the deferral), the picture (stale constants, missing geometry), and no `deferred uniform shadow cannot read` or `inline uniform ... left over / still staged` red lines. Open too: the CPU reads (`rd_cb`'s handles, the index reads of expanded quads, the indirect record reads) still miss an update already copied earlier in the same submission, as before (only kept ones are overlaid); overlaying every unfenced update in `cpu_read` would fix that for every caller (the Metal host's `ubp_get` does that for handles). For the A/B: the scoped barrier does not reset `vka_gbar.c`'s state as the old full one did, so the first game barrier between dispatches after an update is full, not narrow (compare the `game barriers outside a draw pass` line); `mdx_write` still drops the indirect-mesh cache at a kept update, so an indirect mesh draw after one ends the pass; and a fill or update registered in the GPU write list but not recorded (an unaligned fill, a destination outside placed RAM) shows in the shadow as in `gw_content`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (from its logs and Erick's fork; open: the phone A/B).

### A full barrier between the game's compute dispatches on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) a game that runs many small compute dispatches with its own barrier after each loses GPU time to the barriers: `GPU busy` climbs with the dispatches a frame, and the `replay up to frame` lines' `full barriers` per frame stay about one per dispatch once the host's own barrier after each is gone ([A full barrier after every dispatch on the Vulkan replay](#a-full-barrier-after-every-dispatch-on-the-vulkan-replay)).
- **Cause:** every game barrier taken outside a draw pass (`OP_BARRIER` in `vka_exec.c`) was recorded as a full barrier, whatever came before and after it. Between two compute dispatches only the compute stage's shader writes have to reach the next dispatch's reads; upstream measured about 8 µs of Adreno 750 GPU time for each full barrier between 1-group dispatches.
- **Fix:** general (`vka_gbar.c`, the choice in `vka_gbar.h`, copied from Banana Native eden/v0.3.11): a game barrier outside a draw pass is left pending when every command recorded since the last full barrier is a dispatch or a state command, and the next command decides it: before a dispatch, a compute-to-compute barrier (with indirect-command reads when the barrier has `ORDER_INDIRECT_DATA` or the dispatch is indirect); before any other GPU work (a pass's begin, copies, fills, updates, clears, blits, resolves, an image's layout change, a part's or the submission's end), compute writes to `ALL_COMMANDS`; the non-compute work after a narrow barrier still gets that one too. A full barrier recorded meanwhile covers anything pending. To see every command, the vk table's work commands go through `vka_gbar.c` on top of the command encoder (`vka_gbar_install`, after `vka_enc_install` in `vka_dev.c`); this tree has no `vkCmdBeginRenderPass` in its table, so that wrapper is left out (the audit is in `vka_gbar.c`'s comment). `DM_VK_BAR_NARROW=0` (live, default 1) records every game barrier full at once, as before. The statistics add `game barriers outside a draw pass per frame: …`. `t_android_gbar` (`dm/host/tests`) replays every sequence of up to 8 commands against NVN's ordering rule.
- **Check:** built for Android and `t_android_gbar` passes; open: the phone measurement (`DM_VK_BAR_NARROW=0` then `1` in one session; the `compute-to-compute` count should be about the game's barriers between dispatches).
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md) upstream (Banana Native: up to 1,566 1-group dispatches a frame, each followed by the game's barrier); not yet measured on this tree's games.

### Pass-end barriers scoped to the pass's writes on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) GPU time spent at every draw pass end: the `replay up to frame` lines' `full barriers` per frame stay at least the `render passes` per frame, and a pass that a game barrier ends costs two (Ocarina of Time's logs: about 165 passes and 700 full barriers a frame).
- **Cause:** `vka_rp_end` (`vka_draw.c`) ended every pass with a full barrier (`ALL_COMMANDS` to `ALL_COMMANDS`, every write to every read), which on a tiler drains the GPU and flushes the shader write path, also for the many passes whose draws only read memory and write their attachments. `exec`'s `OP_BARRIER` (`vka_exec.c`) then recorded the game's own full barrier after it (merged by `vka_gbar.c` with `DM_VK_BAR_NARROW` on, two with it off).
- **Fix:** general (`vka_draw.c` "Pass-end barriers"; after Erick's Ocarina fork `ac09632` / `986c7fa` / `5aae4d6` / `d7bdee0` and `18ae7be` / `83a225e` / `e30c00a`, without its game gate and without his `FPS_BOOST`, `POSTUB` and UB-flush clauses): a pass none of whose draws wrote memory (`R.swrite` unset: no storage buffer or image, raw global store or geometry-shader claim, `draw_writes`) ends with `vka_attachment_write_barrier` (`vka_tex.c`): source `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT | EARLY_FRAGMENT_TESTS | LATE_FRAGMENT_TESTS` (which takes in every logically earlier stage of both primitive pipelines, so the pass's reads are done before anything after writes over them) with the attachment writes (loadOp CLEAR, `vkCmdClearAttachments`, storeOp STORE), destination unchanged (`ALL_COMMANDS`, MEMORY_READ / MEMORY_WRITE). The full barrier stays when a draw wrote memory, under `DM_VK_NANSCAN`, and at a pass a game barrier ends; that full barrier then stands for the game's (`vka_rp_end_covers_barrier`, `DM_VK_BAR_DEDUP`, live, default 1). A barrier kept inside the pass (`DM_VK_BAR_KEEP`) needs no full end: it is taken only while no draw wrote memory, and the draw `bar_hazard` ends the pass for needs only the execution dependency and the attachment memory scope. The scoped end relies on an invariant written above `vka_rp_end`: every transfer, clear or compute command recorded into the replay's command buffer is followed, before the next pass ends, by a barrier whose source scope covers its stage and its writes, reads included. Two task/mesh fallback sites broke it and are fixed with it (`vka_mesh.inc`): its task and mesh compute runs with the game's bindings and may store to guest memory, which only `mesh_barrier` (the fallback's four buffers) followed, so a batch that may write guest memory (any storage image counts: the translator's image write flag is not followed through image arrays) now records a full barrier in place of `mesh_barrier`, which it covers, so the batch keeps one barrier (`mesh_compute`, `DM_VK_MESH_GUEST_BAR`, live, default 1; this also publishes those writes to the pulling fragment stage of the same draw, which nothing did before); and the controller after a scoped pass end records `mesh_barrier` again (`mesh_control`), whose transfer stage orders `mesh_rep_copy`'s transfer read of the draw record before the controller rewrites it. `DM_VK_RP_SCOPE=0` (live, default 1) ends every pass with the full barrier, as before. `vka_gbar.c` counts a scoped end as a full barrier (`vka_gbar_pass_end`; `DM_VK_RP_SCOPE_NARROW`, live, default 1; not in the reviewed plan, safe for the same reason as the scoped end: pending game barriers are recorded before it, a pass opens only at a draw or a clear, which settle a dispatch's pending barrier, and no dispatch runs in a pass). Without it the scoped end would cost barriers: "pass, SET_RT, OP_BARRIER, dispatch" went from a full end and a merged game barrier to a scoped end and a full one, and "pass, dispatch, OP_BARRIER, dispatch" lost its compute-to-compute narrowing. With `DM_VK_BAR_DEDUP`, the inline updates kept in the pass (`DM_VK_UB_DEFER`) are copied after the full pass end that stands for the game's barrier, so that barrier now precedes them; their own transfer barrier (to `ALL_COMMANDS`, MEMORY_READ / MEMORY_WRITE) publishes them and their sources are fresh scratch. Not ported: Erick's execution-only barrier for a pass that wrote nothing (`25c9a68`): every pass here uses storeOp STORE, an attachment write even where no pixel changed, chosen at `vkCmdBeginRendering` before the draws are known. The replay statistics add `attachment-write barriers` to the `replay up to frame` line and `pass ends per frame: … attachment-scoped (… with a kept game barrier), … full (DM_VK_RP_SCOPE); full barriers saved after a pass-ending game barrier … (DM_VK_BAR_DEDUP); task/mesh fallback batch barriers widened to full for guest writes …`. `dm/tools/tests/test_vk_rp_scope.py` checks the structure and lints the recording sites against the invariant (into the replay's command buffer in `vka_exec.c`, `vka_draw.c` and `vka_mesh.inc`, and into a `cb` parameter in `vka_tex.c`). For an A/B against the old command stream turn off all four switches together: `DM_VK_RP_SCOPE=0` alone keeps the deduplicated barrier and the widened fallback barrier.
- **Check:** the Gradle APK build and the source test; open: the phone measurement (an `nxreplay` capture with `DM_VK_RP_SCOPE=0` then `1` and `DM_VK_BAR_DEDUP=0` then `1` should give the same digests; live, `GPU busy` p50 and the new statistics: most pass ends scoped, the fallback's guest-write barriers non-zero only in task/mesh games), and sync validation if the layer can be pushed (the scoped end relies on the driver taking every logically earlier stage into an attachment-stage-only source scope, as the specification says, including passes with rasterizer discard where only pre-rasterization stages run: not yet seen on the Adreno 8xx proprietary driver or Turnip) (the pool-feedback bitmap's atomics from draws in different passes are no longer ordered against each other: harmless, an idempotent OR read only behind the submission end's barrier). Expected gain small (0 to about 0.5 ms of GPU time a frame; Erick published no A/B), and the proprietary driver may treat both barriers alike.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (from Erick's fork and the code; open: the phone A/B).

### Pixel shaders with nothing to do left out on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) GPU time in depth-only passes (shadow maps, depth pre-passes, occlusion passes): NVN games draw them with a pixel shader bound and every colour write masked, and the tiler bins and shades for that shader although nothing it computes is kept.
- **Cause:** `pstate_fill` (`vka_pso.c`) put every program's pixel shader into its pipeline, whatever the pipeline's colour write masks.
