- **Fix:** general (`vka_pso.c` "Pixel shaders with nothing to do"; after Erick's Ocarina fork `f2b70e5` / `a52a663`, the idea only, without its game gate): a pipeline whose colour writes are all masked (or that has no colour target), without alpha to coverage, leaves out a pixel shader whose only effect is its colour outputs. `vka_spv_fs_inert` (`vka_spvpatch.c`) reads that off the module, as `pf_pass` reads its early-test kinds: no discard, demote or terminate, no depth, sample mask or stencil export (built-in or execution mode), no memory write (a pointer of unknown class counts); a shader whose one discard is the alpha test's (under `DMSH_VK_SPEC_ALPHA_FUNC`) qualifies when the pipeline's alpha test is off or `ALWAYS`. Vulkan then tests and writes depth and counts occlusion samples from the rasterized depth alone, as with the shader. `vka_shader_fs_inert` reads each pixel shader once (the override, the mesh fragment rewrite and the one-lane rewrite applied, as `spv_module` does). The log counts the pipelines (`pixel shaders left out: …`). `DM_VK_FS_ELIDE=0` (read when a pipeline is made, default 1) keeps the shader, as before.
- **Check:** phone A/B 2026-11-16 (POCO F7 Ultra, 891.14, `android_bench.py`, save put back before each run, together with [Depth a pass only reads is not stored](#depth-a-pass-only-reads-is-not-stored-on-the-vulkan-replay)): Ocarina of Time leaves out the pixel shader of 64 pipelines, and the end-of-window pictures are the same (shadows, fog, foliage). GPU time p50 unchanged within noise: by the scarecrow 46.13 / 46.25 ms with both, 46.45 / 46.27 ms without; by Mido 23.11 / 23.17 ms against 23.09 / 23.13 ms. Its frame is held by the task/mesh fallback and the scene passes, not by these passes.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (from Erick's fork: 320 to 478 draws a frame in its 1280x720 depth-only passes on the Adreno 840).

### Depth a pass only reads is not stored on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) every draw pass writes its whole depth attachment back from tile memory at its end, also a pass that loaded the depth and never wrote it (colour passes over a finished depth pre-pass, decals, transparents).
- **Cause:** `rp_begin` (`vka_draw.c`) opened every pass with storeOp STORE, chosen at `vkCmdBeginRendering` before the pass's draws are known.
- **Fix:** general (`vka_draw.c` "Depth the pass only reads"; after Erick's Ocarina fork `b9cd4d6`, which keeps it to Ocarina's 1280x720 passes): a pass whose depth attachment has no stencil aspect, is loaded (no clear at its begin or waiting for it) and whose first draw has depth writes off ends that attachment with storeOp NONE (Vulkan 1.3, which the replay requires): its memory keeps what it had, with nothing written back. NONE leaves the contents undefined if the pass writes depth after all, so a later draw of the pass with depth writes on ends it first (the next pass opens with STORE), and a depth clear inside it is done outside (`vka_rp_clear` refuses it). Not under `DM_VK_NANSCAN`. The replay statistics line counts `passes with their depth not stored per frame` and those ended early. `DM_VK_DEPTH_NONE=0` (live, default 1) stores every pass's depth, as before.
- **Check:** phone A/B 2026-11-16 (as in [Pixel shaders with nothing to do left out](#pixel-shaders-with-nothing-to-do-left-out-on-the-vulkan-replay)): Ocarina of Time ends about 55 of its ~61 passes a frame by the scarecrow, and 35 of 145 by Mido, with the depth not stored; one a frame is ended early by a depth write. Same pictures; GPU time unchanged within noise (the numbers are in that entry): on the Adreno 830 the stores these passes skipped were not what the frame waited for.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (from Erick's fork: two 1280x720 colour passes of 239 to 523 draws test depth without writing it).

### Qualcomm's driver settings file on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) no way to try Qualcomm's own render-mode settings (GMEM or system memory, binning, forced GMEM loads and stores) on a phone: the proprietary driver reads them from `qgl_config.txt` in `/data/vendor/gpu` or `/data/misc/gpu`, which an app cannot write.
- **Cause:** a platform limit; the host loaded the system driver directly and custom drivers through libadrenotools without its file redirect.
- **Fix:** a diagnostic setting (`vka_driver.c` "Qualcomm's driver settings"; after Erick's Ocarina fork `2392d1f`, the idea only): `DM_QGL=<key>=<value>[;…]` writes the file under the app's `DM_TEMP/qgl/data/{vendor,misc}/gpu/` and loads the driver (the system's too) through libadrenotools with `ADRENOTOOLS_DRIVER_FILE_REDIRECT`. `libfile_redirect_hook.so` (`dm/third_party/libadrenotools/src/hook/file_redirect_hook.c`) answers the driver's `fopen`, and `hook_fopen` (`hook_impl.cpp`, changed here) redirects only those two paths, when the copy opens, and leaves every other file the driver's own (upstream redirected every path but `/proc` and `/sys`). Keys: `rendermode` (0 system memory without binning, 1 GMEM with binning, 2 GMEM without, 3 system memory with binning), `forcegmemload`, `forcegmemstore`. A driver that will not load with the redirect loads without it, with a `[RED]` line. Unset: nothing changes. Restart only. The launcher's readiness check opens the driver once for the process before the game starts, so it is given `DM_QGL` and the game's `DM_TEMP` too (`Readiness.driverEnv`, `GameActivity.readinessHold`, `nxlaunch.c`); before, it had only `DM_VK_DRIVER` and `DM_VK_HOOKDIR`, the driver was loaded without the settings and nothing said so. The loader's own lines are kept and written again when the replay opens the driver (`vka_driver_open`): those of the readiness check's open went nowhere, the game log's custom-driver line included.
- **Check:** POCO F7 Ultra, 891.14 (custom), 2026-11-16, `DM_QGL=rendermode=0`: logcat `hook_impl` shows `applied libfile_redirect_hook` and four `hook_fopen: /data/vendor/gpu/qgl_config.txt -> …` (the driver reads the file), the game log the `Qualcomm driver settings` line, and Ocarina of Time draws right by Mido at 22.25 ms of GPU time p50 in one run (23.1 ms in the runs without it, not paired). Paired A/B of `rendermode` (2026-11-16, Ocarina of Time at Link's porch, its heaviest scene, two runs of each, alternating; the driver read the file in every run): the driver's own choice 19.6 / 15.8 fps, `rendermode=0` (system memory without binning) 11.7 / 11.8 fps with the frame at 77-79 ms, `=1` (GMEM with binning) 17.8 / 9.8 fps, `=3` (system memory with binning) 19.9 / 19.9 fps; the scene's main pass 15-17 ms in every mode until the phone heats. No mode is a lever there, and system memory without binning costs a third of the frame rate. Open: the forced GMEM loads and stores, and the system driver's path through adrenotools (not run).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (from Erick's fork, which published no measurement).

### A Qualcomm custom driver silently falls back on Android 15

- **Symptom:** (Android 15 and older, API < 36) the launcher selects a Qualcomm 891.x driver (891.14), but Vulkan reports the system driver's version and features. Logcat's `hook_impl` says `cannot locate symbol "AIBinder_Class_setTransactionCodeToFunctionNameMap"` and `hook_android_dlopen_ext: falling back!`. The game log's `custom GPU driver` line and the custom-driver tag name the requested driver; they do not prove its ICD loaded.
- **Cause:** Qualcomm's 891.x drivers, built for Android 16, import a Binder tracing annotation that `libbinder_ndk.so` exports only from API 36. libadrenotools loads the driver into a linker namespace of its own (`hook_impl.cpp`, parented to the vendor namespace), which preloads only `libhook_impl.so`. The older stub in `libmain_hook.so` is global in the Vulkan loader's namespace, which the driver does not see.
- **Fix:** `libbinder_compat_hook.so` (`android/host/adreno_binder_compat.c`, after Banana Native `f71720e0`): one exported function, plain C, linked `-z global`. `hook_android_dlopen_ext` loads it into the driver's namespace with `RTLD_GLOBAL` before the driver, for a custom driver on API < 36 only; a failure to load it only logs. It forwards the call unchanged to `libbinder_ndk.so` when the system has the function; otherwise only the optional transaction-name annotation is omitted, and no Binder transaction is replaced. Android 16 and the system driver load exactly as before. Upstream built the shim into `hook_impl` with `-z global`; here it is a library of its own, because that would have put `hook_impl`'s statically linked libc++ exports (about 388 symbols) into every driver namespace's global group on every API level. No env switch.
- **Check:** upstream, Odin 3 (Adreno 830, Android 15, 2026-11-11): the unmodified 891.14 changes from Vulkan 1.3.284 / `0x80320048` (the silent system fallback) to Vulkan 1.3.351 / `0x8037b00e`. Here (2026-11-17): built with NDK r30 (the library has the GLOBAL flag and exports the one function); `test_android_binder_compat.py` checks forwarding of every argument, the absent-API case, one lookup, the gate and the build target. **Open:** a run on an Android 15 phone (expect logcat `hook_impl: hook_android_dlopen_ext: applied libbinder_compat_hook (API 35)` and the driver's own version); Android 13 and 14 get the library too, and whether 891.x needs anything else there is unknown. The POCO F7 Ultra (Android 16) never loads it.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (upstream, Odin 3; ported here before any report from our testers).

### A custom GPU driver that silently falls back to the system's

- **Symptom:** (Android) the log says `custom (DM_VK_DRIVER)` but the driver line names the phone's own driver (its build, date and version), and crashes are the system driver's.
- **Cause:** libadrenotools hands back the system's `libvulkan` when the custom library does not load, logging only to logcat; the host cannot tell from the device's entry points (Android's loader answers with its own trampolines).
- **Fix:** after the device is made, `vka_driver_mapped` (`vka_driver.c`, `vka_driverpath.h`, `t_android_driverpath.c`) reads `/proc/self/maps` for the custom library's file; when it is not mapped, `[RED] custom GPU driver … did not take`. From Banana Native v0.3.43 (community PR 409, MIT).
- **Found on:** upstream (AYN Thor Max, Android 13, three Qualcomm driver packages). Phone check pending here.

### Texture uploads deswizzled one after another on the replay thread on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) loading frames of 50 ms to several seconds whose `[longframe]` line names texture first uploads (1-93 a frame in the stutter runs) and no pipelines.
- **Cause:** every texture filled from its storage ([Texture content from storage on the Vulkan replay](#texture-content-from-storage-on-the-vulkan-replay)) is deswizzled into scratch memory on the replay thread, level after level and layer after layer, before the frame's recording goes on.
- **Fix:** (2026-11-03, not phone-checked) a block-linear texture of at least `DM_VK_UPLOAD_MT_MIN` bytes (default 256 KB) has its pieces cut into runs of block rows (`nvn_deswizzle_rows`, at least ~64 KB each) that `DM_VK_UPLOAD_MT` helper threads (`vk-upload`, default 3, live; 0 = the replay thread only) and the replay thread claim by compare-and-swap; the copies are recorded afterwards in the same order (a piece that cannot be deswizzled ends them there, as on the serial path). `t_tex` checks that a level decoded in parts equals one decoded whole; the claim and completion core passed 200,000 batches under ThreadSanitizer. Open: the time saved, from `texture uploads … MB / … ms` in the `[longframe]` lines of a cold area entry with 3 and 0.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49 (the stutter runs' loading frames).

### Images filled from pool storage hold texture bytes twice on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) the process's graphics memory grows with every texture the game loads by about the texture's size twice: `nxctl.sh stats` in DKB's level printed `images 818 MB (7,217; …, from storage 638 MB)` beside 2,727 MB of placed pools.
- **Cause:** the replay keeps each texture in an image of its own (optimal tiling, not the Tegra block-linear layout), filled from its storage in the pool ([Texture content from storage on the Vulkan replay](#texture-content-from-storage-on-the-vulkan-replay)); the storage stays in the pool, which is guest memory the game owns.
- **Fix:** none yet (open). Binding such an image onto its storage's own pool memory would give the copy back, but the image's layout would overwrite the guest's block-linear bytes: a tracked texture the CPU rewrites in part (streamed mip levels, a glyph cache) would then be re-uploaded from bytes that are no longer block-linear, so only content no CPU write follows (cleared, filled once) is a candidate, and only where the driver's requirements fit the storage (the pools' memory type, a size within the storage, its offset aligned). The ctl stats line is now followed by `ctl: images not GPU-written: cleared … MB (n, … MB would fit their storage), filled once …, tracked …; GPU-written … MB` (`vka_tex_alias_stats`) to size that.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49 (RSS 8.07 GB in the level, 638 MB of it images that copy pool storage).

### Integer vertex formats into float inputs on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) `[RED] vk: vertex attribute format 0x27 feeds a float input` and geometry whose integer attributes (bone indices, packed data) are wrong.
- **Cause:** the translator declares every vertex input a float `vec4`; a shader that uses one as integer bit-casts it, and Maxwell's fetch puts the integer itself in the register. Vulkan feeds a float input only a float, normalized or scaled format: a USCALED stand-in hands over the bits of the converted float (5 → `0x40a00000`).
- **Fix:** the replay rewrites the pipeline's vertex module (`vka_spv_vin_retype`, `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_spvpatch.c`): those inputs become `uvec4` / `ivec4`, each load bit-cast back to float, and the attribute keeps its integer format. `t_android_spvpatch` validates the rewrite with spirv-val. `nvn_mtl.m` reaches the same bits by converting on the CPU (`vconv_int`).
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md): formats 0x27, 0x0F, 0x17 and 0x03 (RGBA8UI, RG8UI, RG32UI, R8UI).

### GPU time counted the wait for the recording on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) `[longframe]` lines with a GPU time of 200-980 ms (or 1-4 s) and no new pipeline, no texture upload and nothing else to explain them; in frames that did make pipelines the GPU time equals the frame time and the pipelines' time.
- **Cause:** the replay timed each submission with one timestamp at the top of its first part and one at the bottom of its last (`vka_exec.c`), but a submission goes to the queue in parts while it is still being recorded (`DM_VK_CHUNK`, `DM_VK_CHUNK_PASS`). Between two parts the GPU waits for the replay thread; a pipeline compiled, a texture deswizzled or any other stall of the replay thread in the middle of a submission was counted as GPU time, and CPU stalls looked GPU-bound.
- **Fix:** a timestamp pair per part (two queries per part, `NCHUNK + 1` parts a slot); the frame's GPU time is the sum of the parts' busy times (overlaps merged), and the span (first top to last bottom) is kept beside it. The `[longframe]` line prints both: `GPU 2.1 ms (busy; span 410.0 ms, 407.9 waiting for the recording)` is a replay-thread stall, not GPU work. The overlay's GPU time and the ADPF reports read the busy time too.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49 (Adreno 840): warm-cache runs with 2-12 "GPU" spikes of 200-980 ms and 0 new pipelines; in a 425 s session every frame that made pipelines showed a GPU time equal to the pipelines' creation time (e.g. 1,457 ms frame, 1,451 ms "GPU", 1,397 ms making 15 pipelines). Open: what the remaining spikes are once they are measured this way.

### Pipelines made on the frame path on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) stutters of 50 ms to several seconds in a level, each `[longframe]` line naming new pipelines (`new pipelines 15 …, 1396.6 ms in all`), worst on a first visit to an area, after a driver change or with an empty pipeline cache; per pipeline 30-60 ms at full clocks and 150-500 ms with the CPU thermally capped at 1 GHz. With graphics pipeline libraries on (`DM_VK_GPL`, default) cold runs were no better, and the `draws with a hybrid pipeline` count was always 0.
- **Cause:** a pipeline the map lacks is made on the replay thread at the draw that needs it, and the frame's recording waits for it. Three things kept that from improving: (1) Qualcomm's Adreno 840 driver (build 718a07726a) refused every pre-rasterization library with `VK_ERROR_UNKNOWN` (-13): no pipeline was ever linked, and each miss paid the cache-only try, a vertex-input library and a refused library before the whole pipeline (70 ms a pipeline against 59 without GPL in comparable cold runs); (2) the hybrid count was a Mac stub on Android (`android_stub.c`, always 0), so it could not show it; (3) `DM_VK_PCACHE=0`, meant as "no cache", was read as a file named `0` in the working folder, so a second "cold" run read the first one's pipelines back. The cache was saved once a minute from the replay thread, `vkGetPipelineCacheData` (17 MB) under the lock the pipeline lookups take.
- **Fix:** (`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_pso.c`) with `DM_VK_GPL_PROBE=1` (default 0: it makes ~11-13 libraries on the replay thread, a 1-6 s hitch, so it is a one-off diagnostic run) the first refused pre-rasterization library runs a probe that makes it again with one thing changed at a time (no pipeline cache, `RETAIN_LINK_TIME_OPTIMIZATION_INFO`, no `VkPipelineRenderingCreateInfo`, no dynamic state, no specialization constants, no subgroup pins, the first stage only, an independent-sets layout, together with the fragment shader, everything simplified) and logs each result in one line (`graphics pipeline library probe …`); an adoptable change that makes it is used for the run, and with none GPL turns itself off after `DM_VK_GPL_GIVEUP` refusals (default 3) with no library made (`graphics pipeline libraries: off for this run`), so a miss costs the whole pipeline only. `draws with a hybrid pipeline` now counts draws recorded with a linked pipeline. A **prewarm list** (`vk_pipelines.keys` beside the cache: shader keys and state bytes, not driver code) records each pipeline the frame path makes; the next run makes them again on `DM_VK_PREWARM` background threads (default 2, nice 10) from its first pipeline on, so the frame path finds them made, and it waits for a prewarm thread making the same key instead of making it twice (`waited for prewarm` in the `[longframe]` line). The cache and the list are saved `DM_VK_PCACHE_SAVE_S` seconds (default 20) after the last save on a saver thread, without the lookups' lock; `DM_VK_PCACHE=0` keeps no file.
- **Then (review, 2026-11-03):** the cache save tries `vkGetPipelineCacheData` up to 3 times with a quarter more room (the cache grows between the size query and the copy while prewarm adds pipelines; a `VK_INCOMPLETE` copy used to drop the save, the forced one at suspend too) and writes only a complete one; a frame path waiting for a prewarm thread building the same key raises that thread to its own priority for the build (it ran at nice 10: a priority inversion); the prewarm and optimizer threads pause on suspend (`mtl_drain`) until the next submission; the keys file is `NXVKPW2` with a fingerprint of `vka_gkey`'s layout and every record is range-checked before use (`pw_rec_ok`); `DM_VK_PREWARM_KEEP` applies when the list is written, keeping the newest records (the cap counted the loaded records, so a full list never took a new key); two threads making the same retyped vertex module keep one.
- **Then (2026-11-08, ported from upstream Banana Native PR #12 and v0.1.2; a stopgap, off by default):** `DM_VK_PSO_ASYNC=<n>` (restart; at most 4 threads) takes the graphics pipeline misses off the frame: the cache-only try stays on the frame path (counted as the frame's), and a miss is made on one of n background threads (`vk-psoasync`, nice 10, paused with the others on suspend; the frame path's own build: linked from libraries, optimized later by `vk-psopt`) while every draw that needs it is skipped (no pipeline: the draw is dropped, nothing waits with a deadline and nothing stands in), as Eden's asynchronous shaders do. A key a prewarm thread is making is skipped too instead of waited for. A skipped draw is missing from that frame only, unless the game keeps what it drew (a texture or shadow map drawn once and reused), which stays wrong until it is drawn again. With GPL off (`DM_VK_GPL=0`, or a driver that refused its libraries) there is no cache-only try, so even a pipeline the cache holds goes to the background and blinks for a frame or two. Compute and mesh pipelines stay on the frame path. The `pipelines up to frame` line adds `asynchronous: … queued, … made (… ms in all, longest wait from the miss … ms), deepest queue …, draws skipped waiting …`, the `[longframe]` line `draws skipped for background pipelines …` (that frame's), and the prewarm-miss lines say `pipeline made in the background` (its time counted from the miss: how long its draws were skipped) instead of `on the frame path`. Upstream measured it on an AYN Thor (Adreno 740, Turnip 26.3.0-T30, 1152x648) in DKB's first level: without it, 96 pipelines on the frame path in about 40 s, 13.7 s of waits, frames up to 1.56 s; with `=2`, 131 made in the background, the longest skip 426 ms, no frame of play over 300 ms, 30 fps held. With it came a race fix in `lib_make`: it looked a library key up, unlocked and made it, so two linking threads that missed the same part (one fragment shader under many vertex shaders) both compiled it and the second map entry leaked the first library; now the first claims the key (`LIB_BUSY`) and the others wait on `g_lib_cv`. Only the replay thread linked before, so the race needed the asynchronous threads (prewarm and the optimizer make whole pipelines). A part made while the probe's adaptation emptied its map is not stored (each map has a generation), so it cannot overwrite the claim of a thread making it again with the adaptation. The cause, slow pipeline compiles on the phone's drivers, is not addressed. Measured 2026-11-08 on Ocarina of Time (POCO F7 Ultra, Qualcomm 891.11, title to Link's house, two runs each, [its note](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#open)), it stays off by default: on an empty pipeline cache and no prewarm list (`DM_VK_PCACHE=0`, a prewarm file that does not exist), `=0` made 94 graphics pipelines in one frame on entering the house (35.2 s and 35.0 s, about 375 ms each), then frames of 6 s and 14 s, then presented nothing for over 2 minutes (0.0 and 0.3 fps); `=2` kept the game and its HUD running but drew only the HUD over a black scene for 5 to 7 minutes: 500 / 517 queued, 288 / 297 made (145.8 s / 190.4 s, about 0.5 to 0.64 s each), the longest wait from the miss 63.2 s / 84.4 s, the queue up to 319 deep, 285,308 / 240,121 draws skipped. Its frames of 12.8 to 15.2 s that remained were compute pipelines (92 of the 129 the frame path made; in one run 37 of them over 100 ms, 49.7 s in all, the slowest 5.9 s), which it does not take. With the warm cache (36 MB, 892 prewarm keys, 891 made in 0.8 s) `=2` queued nothing and the picture was correct (37.4 to 38.2 fps in the house; the drops on camera turns were paging, 16,000 to 31,000 major faults a frame, with 0 new pipelines). So on this driver it trades a freeze for a black scene on a cold cache and does nothing on a warm one: neither is playable, and what makes the cold case playable is the prewarm list and the cache. Open: Turnip; the compute pipelines on the frame path.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49 (Adreno 840), stutter runs of 2026-11-02/03: 897 pipelines made on the frame path in a 425 s session, 44 s in all; in cold runs 478-488 pipelines and 29.6-35.6 s. A shader library change does not invalidate the cache by itself: between libraries `9b3a2294056a` and `e38a586a8a89` 22,824 of 22,852 shaders kept byte-identical SPIR-V (28 compute shaders changed). Open: the probe's answer on the phone; whether the driver compiles before answering `FAIL_ON_PIPELINE_COMPILE_REQUIRED` (the `cache-only tries … missed (… ms in the misses)` count in the pipelines line).

### Pipelines compiled with the game closed on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) the first minutes of a game with a cold pipeline cache (a new install, another GPU driver, a system update that brought a new driver) stop for seconds at a time: the prewarm list's background threads compile while the game plays and compete with it for the CPU, the memory and the driver, and a draw whose pipeline they have not reached yet compiles it on the frame path. Ocarina of Time on the POCO F7 Ultra (891.14), cold: the prewarm took 986 s for its 3,613 keys.
- **Cause:** the prewarm (`vka_pso.c` `pw_main`) ran only inside the game's process, from its first pipeline on.
- **Fix:** general (`vka_pso.c` "Warming with the game closed", `vka_warm.c`, `android/app` `WarmActivity` and `Warm`; the idea from SCSKiller, docs/ANALISE-SCSKILLER-2026-11-16.md, nothing of its code): Play first opens a screen in a process of its own (`:warm`) when the game's last complete warm was not for this GPU driver, shader library, host build and system build (`Warm.wanted`: `temp/<title ID>/pipelines.warm`). It calls `nxhost_vk_warm` with the game's environment as far as it decides the cache and its pipelines (`Warm.env`: `DM_TEMP`, the game's graphics settings, its driver, `env.txt` and the game's own lines): the pack's profile is read, the pack folder is the module folder (`dm_module_dir_set`), the Vulkan device comes up with no guest memory, and `vka_pso_warm` runs the same prewarm list, threads and gates as the game's prewarm with `DM_VK_PREWARM_CONCURRENCY` at 4, saving the cache now and then and at the end. The game starts when it ends. Play now (`nxhost_vk_warm_stop`) ends it after the compiles under way, keeps what was made and starts the game, whose own prewarm does the rest as before. A scripted start (`--ez run true`, `android_bench.py`) does not warm unless `--ez warm true`. The process ends with the screen, so the warm's device and the driver's memory are gone before the game's process starts.
- **Check:** POCO F7 Ultra, Ocarina of Time, 891.14, the test app's cache set aside: the warm made 3,227 of 3,613 keys (139 already in the cache, 247 seed keys dropped as in every run) in 986 s with four compiles at once (7 GB available); the game started by itself after it, and its own prewarm went through the 3,613 keys in 4.5 s, with one pipeline made on the frame path. Open: the stutter count of a cold first session with and without the warm, and Turnip (one compile at a time there).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md).

### Prewarm made the oldest records first on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) with a prewarm list of more than 8,192 records (`prewarm list …: N pipeline keys of earlier runs`), the scenes of the latest sessions still make pipelines on the frame path (`[longframe]` lines naming new pipelines; `pipeline made on the frame path …, not in the prewarm list: … the same key (the prewarm threads had not reached it yet)`), and the prewarm line counts `8192 of N`.
- **Cause:** `pw_main` (`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_pso.c`) walked the loaded records from index 0. The list grows at its end (the loaded records in file order, then each run's new ones) and `pw_save` drops the oldest, so index 0 is the oldest record; the walk also stops at `DM_VK_PREWARM_MAX` (8,192) while the list keeps `DM_VK_PREWARM_KEEP` (16,384). Past 8,192 records the newest were never made ahead, and below that the oldest sessions' pipelines came first.
- **Fix:** the prewarm threads take the loaded records from the last one back, so the cap leaves out the oldest. **Open:** a key used in every run but first recorded long ago keeps its old place (the frame path notes only the pipelines it makes itself, and a prewarmed key is found made), so it ages toward the front and the cap drops it eventually; it is noted again the next time the frame path makes it.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 42,321 shaders, and about 5,300 distinct pipelines in 25 minutes in Lichii, which made the same change (frizti-v3, `7b519498`). From code reading; not yet measured on the phone.

### Linked pipelines kept after their optimized pipeline replaced them on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) the driver's host memory (`DM_VK_HOSTMEM=1`, `driver host memory through the allocator`) grows with every pipeline a run links from stage libraries and does not come back once the optimized pipeline is made, while the `pipelines up to frame` line counts the same number linked and optimized in the background.
- **Cause:** a pipeline the cache lacks is linked from its stage libraries for the draw that waits for it, and its optimized pipeline is made later by `vk-psopt` (`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_pso.c` `opt_main`). That thread stored the optimized pipeline in the map entry over the linked one and never destroyed the linked one, so each linked VkPipeline, and the driver memory it held, stayed to the end of the process. The stage libraries are not part of it: they are kept for other links.
- **Fix:** `linked_retire` destroys the linked pipeline the optimized one replaced, once no submission can still use it. The replay may have read it from the entry just before the swap, into the submission it is recording, whose fence is reserved before that submission or at its commit. So the destroy waits for the fence after the last one reserved at the swap (`vka_after`), then for the fence after the last one reserved when that one completed. After a GPU hang, when fences read as done, the pipeline is kept. Handle reuse is safe: the bound pipeline the draws compare with (`DY.pipe`) is cleared at every command buffer. The `pipelines up to frame` line adds `(N linked ones destroyed)`. It counts only where pipelines are linked: a cold pipeline cache, a new driver, a new scene. A warm run takes its pipelines from the cache and links none.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (POCO F7 Ultra, 2026-11-22, Kokiri Forest with the camera turning):
  - Cold cache (`DM_VK_PCACHE=0 DM_VK_PREWARM=0`), Turnip V39: 35 linked, 35 optimized and 35 destroyed, no crash or hang.
  - The device's cache: on 891.14, 2 linked and 2 destroyed; on V39, none linked.
  - The memory saved was not measured: too few pipelines were linked in these runs.
- **Check:** the `pipelines up to frame` line with `DM_VK_PCACHE=0`: the linked count equals the destroyed count once the optimizer has caught up.

### Lookups waiting for a pool placement on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) in level loads, `[longframe]` lines of 100-550 ms whose game waited that long on GPU fences (`waits: GPU fences 514.6`) with 3-4 ms of GPU work, and `replay recording` as long as the fence wait; others of 300 ms to 1.6 s with no fence wait, no CPU on the frame's thread and high process CPU (`unexplained` in `longframes.py`). Both come in stretches where hundreds of MB of memory pools become placed Vulkan memory.
- **Cause:** `mtl_pool_add` (`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_mem.c`) held the segment table's write lock across the whole placement: the Vulkan allocation, the copy of the range's bytes, the placed map and the prefault, about 0.7 ms a MiB on the Adreno 840 (a 500 MiB pool: 350 ms). Every address lookup that missed its cache meanwhile (`vka_addr`: the replay thread's vertex, uniform and texture addresses, and the game's own NVN calls; each new segment invalidates the caches, so misses are frequent in a load) waited for the lock: the replay thread stood in one submission, and the game in its fence wait for it or in its NVN call.
- **Fix:** a placement mutex serializes placements and the table's edits; the write lock is taken only around each edit (an insert, a reference count, a removal). A pool's range enters the table only when its placement ends, and no command uses a pool before `mtl_pool_add` returns, so no lookup misses a segment it needs. The `[longframe]` line counts `pool placement x ms (n pools, MB), give-back y ms` and `the pool table's lock waited for: replay x, others y`; `replay recording x (its CPU y)` tells a blocked replay thread from a busy one. `DM_VK_MEM_LOCK_WHOLE=1` restores the old locking for an A/B.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49 (Turnip v32): the level load at 36-50 s of the second warm run of 2026-11-03 (1.98 → 2.51 GiB placed in 1.6 s; fence waits of 515, 298, 168, 122 ms with GPU 2.5-3.9 ms). Not yet phone-checked.
- **Then (phone runs, 2026-11-03):** with the lock fixed the load still had one 1.6-2.3 s frame and several of 200-530 ms, now named by the new attribution: the render thread in a guest wait ended by the main thread, the game's `StageScene` / `al::Init` loading threads busiest, and `pool placement` 1.2-1.8 s in that frame for 231 pools of 7.9 MB. The placement's phases (`placed memory: N placements: allocation …, protections read …`) put 7.5 s of 9.8 s in reading `/proc/self/maps` (1-5 ms a read: the text runs from the first of the process's thousands of mappings). The kernel's `PROCMAP_QUERY` ioctl (per address) is refused to apps (SELinux, EACCES). The protections now come from a snapshot of the text, read again only for a range whose 256 KiB blocks changed since: this library's `mmap`, `munmap`, `mprotect` and `mremap` (defined over the system calls, so every host caller binds to them) mark the blocks they touch after the call; a swap leaves its range's protections as they were, so its own calls (the system calls, unmarked) and the driver's placed map do not mark, unless a snapshot read ran during any part of it, a call failed, or the range's runs were not all known (then it marks the range at its end); a block's generation only rises (an atomic maximum). Answers from the snapshot are checked against the text, the first 16 and then every 64th (a difference turns the snapshot off, `[RED]` with the range and the first run that differs); a failed or truncated read leaves no snapshot. A snapshot whose read began at generation g0 is stale only for marks above g0 (a mark that took g0 or less was made before the read). A difference is looked at twice (another thread's call can be in the text before its mark), and 8 failed reads in a row turn the snapshot off. Placements wait for pending give-backs of overlapping segments (the give-back runs after the placement lock is dropped, and its free took a new placed range there down). Before a swap maps its range again where the driver may have taken it down, it reserves the range (`MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE`) and says so (`[RED]`) when another mapping took part of it; the window between the driver's `vkFreeMemory` and the move of the anonymous pages stays open on the Adreno driver (its free takes down the range reserved by `VK_MEMORY_UNMAP_RESERVE_BIT_EXT`), and `DM_VK_UNPLACE_REMAP=1` is an untested experiment for it. Placements drop the placement lock while they wait. Open (older, narrow): a protection the dirty tracker changes in a pool's range between the swap's read of the runs and its end would be put back as read; tracked pages lie inside live pools and swaps touch only uncovered gaps and unreferenced segments, so it remains only for a give-back over a live pool (a game error), a tracked pool whose placement failed beside a new one when the placement alignment exceeds a page, and the guest's permission calls on pool memory (comment at `prot_known`, `vka_mem.c`). The give-back's reservation check reads the text when the range is occupied after the free; a driver that keeps its reservations (all of the first 4 give-backs find the whole range `PROT_NONE`) is learned once, logged with its line of the text, and its give-backs skip the text from then on. Protections read: 7,467 → 166 ms for 2,048 placements; the load's longest frame 1,616-2,271 → 233 ms. `DM_VK_MAPS_CACHE=0` reads the text each time.

### Textures of two formats over one image on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) `[RED] vk: image VkFormat 37 (NVN 0x25, 2304x1296) asked for a view as VkFormat 122, which its format list does not have: viewed in its own format`.
- **Cause:** textures of one memory and shape share one image (`same_shape`, `vka_tex.c`) when their formats have the same block size, so content passes between them whatever the operation (draws, copies, clears, presents, write-back). Since the images keep Adreno's compression with a two-entry format list (own format and sRGB twin) instead of `MUTABLE_FORMAT`, a sharing texture of another format cannot have a view of it: its views fell back to the image's format. DKB reuses the memory of an RGBA8 texture for an R11G11B10 HDR target, which was then drawn and sampled as RGBA8 UNORM (values clamped to [0, 1] at 8 bits).
- **Fix:** a texture shares the image only when the image's format list takes its formats (storage and view). Otherwise it gets its own image of its format, and the pair (image format, other format, size) is learned: every image made from then on with that format and size takes the other format in its list (`MUTABLE_FORMAT`; that image loses UBWC) and is shared, and the pairs are kept in `$DM_TEMP/vk_view_formats.txt`, so the next run shares from the first use. A view asked in a format outside the list (a texture view's own format) is learned the same way and falls back to the image's format for that image's life. Without `DM_TEMP`, once the 64-pair table is full, or past 4 learned formats for one image format and size, the textures stay on separate images (lost in that run: a bit-for-bit reinterpretation between the two formats through the memory, which the format list never allowed either). Only pairs of uncompressed colour formats of one texel size are learned or read from the file (a format list may hold only formats compatible with the image's); the file is written whole through a temporary file. Compressed textures of different formats over one memory (BC1 and BC4, or BC3, BC5, BC7 and ASTC 4x4, which have one block size) never share an image: no view reinterprets one as the other. `DM_VK_VIEW_LEARN=0` learns and reads nothing.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md): once a run in the level (86 s into the second run of 2026-11-03; 1536x864 in the first).


### Integer shader images over float textures on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) a compute pass that stores packed words into a colour target leaves it unwritten: `[RED] vk: shader … binding …: texture ID … has no view of shape 0x81 (storage): it reads as unbound (… format 0x25 …)`, and `[RED] vk: image VkFormat 37 (NVN 0x25, …) asked for a view as VkFormat 98, which its format list does not have` that comes back every run. The picture the pass produces stays black.
- **Cause:** the shader declares a `uint` (or `int`) storage image and the texture bound there is a float format of 4-byte texels (RGBA8; agl's upscaler stores its output as packed `R32_UINT` words, which the Switch's typed store converts by the descriptor's format). `id_view` (`vka_tex.c`) refused a view whose class differs from the shader's, and the R32_UINT view could not be learned either: [the learned view formats](#textures-of-two-formats-over-one-image-on-the-vulkan-replay) took only float / unorm formats (kind 0), so `vl_pair_ok` dropped RGBA8 → R32_UINT although Vulkan puts both in one compatibility class (by texel size). Every run fell back the same way.
- **Fix:** pairs of uncompressed colour formats of one texel size are learned whatever their class (float, uint, sint). An integer storage binding over a float texture of 4-byte texels takes an `R32_UINT` / `R32_SINT` view of its image (the same bits) once the image's format list has it: the first such binding learns the pair (`texture serial … bound as an integer shader image: its image has no VkFormat 98 view; learned …`), and the images of that format and size made from then on (and in the next runs, from `$DM_TEMP/vk_view_formats.txt`) take it. An image without storage use is remade with it (`img_storage_upgrade`), and the remade image takes the pairs learned by then (the swap now carries its format list over too): one whose own format has storage support is remade and bound at the learning binding itself; one whose own format has none (B10G11R11_UFLOAT on Adreno) is remade at the learning binding when no render pass is open (a dispatch, or the draw's first use of the image, which `make_images` makes before its pass), and otherwise stays unbound that once, because the draw's pass was not ended for a remake, and is remade and bound at its next binding (`storage_able` counts a learned format with storage support, so `vka_tex_id_storage_pending` ends the pass). `img_storage_upgrade` never copies inside a render pass (`vka_tex_feedback` tells `vka_tex.c` whether one is open): a later binding in the same draw's descriptor writes, which finds the pair just learned (the same image bound twice, or another image of that format and size), stays unbound too instead of breaking `VUID-vkCmdCopyImage-renderpass`. A storage binding whose view would be in a format without storage support (a float storage binding of such an image, in its own format) is unbound with `[RED] vk: texture serial … bound as a shader image in VkFormat …, which has no storage support: unbound` (once a pair), instead of a view without storage use (`VUID-VkWriteDescriptorSet-descriptorType-00339`). An image that already has storage use keeps its format list, and such a binding stays unbound for its life. The pair is learned before the remake: at first `id_view` tried the remake first, which `storage_able` refused for a format without storage support, and returned before learning, so the B10G11R11 → R32_UINT pair (`122 98`) never came on its own, only from a hand-written `vk_view_formats.txt`. An image whose own format has no storage support but a learned format that has gets storage use through `VK_IMAGE_CREATE_EXTENDED_USAGE_BIT`, and only such an image gets that flag: the other mutable-format images (sRGB twins, learned float views, everything under `DM_VK_UBWC=0`) keep the uses of their own format, so their compression and format-property answers are what they were. A view keeps storage use exactly when its format supports it (an sRGB view, or the own-format view of such an image, leaves it out). sRGB textures with the IMAGE flag get storage use on their image (it is made in the UNORM format, which has storage support; a storage binding views it in that format, the raw bits a Maxwell / Ampere surface store writes): Zelda's NDVE final pass (`4f41ccf24f163e0d`) stores into texture 1931, made `RGBA8_SRGB` (`0x38`), through an RGBA8 UNORM view, and with sRGB textures left out its image had no storage use, `storage_able` refused the remake and that binding stayed unbound. Still open: the views of an image made before the pair was learned (the first scene of the first run), float storage of formats without storage support (`E5B9G9R9`), and targets whose size changes with dynamic resolution (pairs are kept per size, so each new size is learned once).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (POCO F7 Ultra, Qualcomm 891.11, 2026-11-07): agl's upscaler (`agl_ndsr_vr.pck` `d17eefaff4f0bc23`) wrote its 1536x864 RGBA8 output through such bindings; gameplay after the prologue was black at 16-26 fps with 600-800 draws a frame. With the pairs learned the scene shows (Link's house, 29-31 fps), though overexposed (open). That run's `vk_view_formats.txt` had the `122 98` pair written by hand; the learning before the remake, the `EXTENDED_USAGE` gate, the render-pass guard and the storage-format check are compiled for Android (NDK r30) and not yet run on the phone with that file removed.

### Captures whose textures shared another format's image on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) `nxreplay` stops before the frame: `[RED] nxreplay: texture ser=… : captured as 1536x864 VkFormat 122, … ; this build makes it 1536x864 VkFormat 123, … (the replay's image creation changed)`, on a capture made with [learned view formats](#textures-of-two-formats-over-one-image-on-the-vulkan-replay) in use.
- **Cause:** in the live run the texture shared the image of a texture of another format over the same storage and shape (made before it, and whose format list takes its format), so the capture wrote its content with that image's format. The replayer fills the captured images in serial order, not in the order the live run made them: the texture came first, took an image of its own format, and the content could not go in. The replayer also leaves out the captured `DM_TEMP`, so without a copy of the learned pairs no image takes another format at all.
- **Fix:** `fill_tex` (`vka_replay.c`) makes the image first through the texture of the captured format over the same storage and shape (a record or a registration of the capture; else a stand-in texture of that format), so the texture shares it as it did live, and says so (`texture ser=… : its live image was VkFormat …, made first through texture ser=…`). Replays of such captures take `DM_TEMP=<folder>` with a copy of the app's `vk_view_formats.txt` (`android/TESTING.md`).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (2026-11-07, POCO F7 Ultra): its gameplay captures (Link's house) had agl's `E5B9G9R9` upscaler input over the `R11G11B10F` scene target, and a mid-frame capture an `R32F` texture over an `R11G11B10F` one of the transient heap; both replay now.

### GPU-produced indirect draw counts on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) objects driven by compute-generated draw counts disappear or use a previous count; possible vegetation flicker. A count produced earlier in the same GPU submission is still stale when command recording reads it on the CPU.
- **Cause:** `vka_draw` read `mdi_cnt` through `cpu_read` and discarded each subdraw before the queued producer had executed. A GPU memory barrier cannot make a later GPU write available to an earlier CPU read during recording.
- **Fix:** record full-width maximum and stride in the NVN draw payload and emit one `vkCmdDrawIndirectCount` / `vkCmdDrawIndexedIndirectCount` for the group. Subsequent legacy subrecords remain for Metal's masking path and are ignored by Vulkan. Query and enable `drawIndirectCount` and `multiDrawIndirect`, load both entry points, validate limits/alignment/ranges, and resolve both buffers with indirect usage. The existing producer barriers cover writes to indirect-command reads; no per-draw CPU wait/readback is added. Unsupported devices report the missing capability and skip; a GPU-masking fallback remains open. Capture layout fingerprints and argument extents include the new metadata.
- **Validation:** `t_capture` covers full-width stride and full-group argument capture; `vka_selftest` runs 16 pixel-checked cases when indirect count is supported and at least 17 frames are requested: arrays/indexed, packed/70,000-byte stride, counts 0/1/3/5 with maximum 3, alternating counter buffers. A GPU copy overwrites an intentionally stale counter in the same submission before each draw. Further coverage of compute producers, encoder off and validation layers remains required.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md#swap2-native-on-the-poco-f7-ultra-2026-11-05), POCO F7 Ultra / Turnip: native path reached with maximum 76 and stride 20. The ordering bug is fixed; complete vegetation correctness remains open because storage-image bindings and mesh support still fail independently.

### Indirect NVN2 mesh draws on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferDrawMeshTasksIndirect`, and what the game draws that way is missing (in Zelda: Ocarina of Time, by ocarina-recomp's account, the culled foliage and garden hedges).
- **Cause:** only the direct `nvnCommandBufferDrawMeshTasks` was recorded; the indirect form stayed on the stub. Its record is NVIDIA's `{count, first}` (the layout of `VkDrawMeshTasksIndirectCommandNV`), and a culling compute writes it earlier in the frame, so the CPU cannot read it at replay. `VK_EXT_mesh_shader` reads `{x, y, z}`: passed in place, `y = first` (0) draws nothing.
- **Fix:** `hle_nvn.c` records it as a mesh draw (`NVN_PRIM_MESH`) carrying the record's address (`p_draw.ind`). `vka_draw.c` `mesh_ind_args` takes a 12-byte span of the submission's scratch (the scratch blocks now have indirect usage), where a one-thread compute (`selftest/meshcount.comp`) writes `{count, 1, 1}` outside any pass, then a full barrier. A count past the device's limit per dimension (`maxTaskWorkGroupCount` / `maxMeshWorkGroupCount`, 65535 on the Adreno 840; a larger one is undefined in `VK_EXT_mesh_shader`) becomes 0, as the direct draw skips one. Without push descriptors the CPU writes `{0, 1, 1}` and the GPU copies `count` over `x` (`vkCmdCopyBuffer`, 4 bytes), unchecked (a red line once). The draw is `vkCmdDrawMeshTasksIndirectEXT(span, 1, 12)` through the command encoder. Whatever wrote the record already ended with a barrier (dispatches, buffer copies, passes). A device without `VK_EXT_mesh_shader` skips it with a red line, as the direct draw does; there is no fallback there (no vertex-shader form of the mesh programs exists, points or triangles). The capture takes the 8-byte record with the draw.
- **Open:**
  - `first` (the base workgroup) is not applied, as for the direct draw: the translated task and mesh shaders read their workgroup index with no base. The replay logs the CPU's view of the record (an earlier GPU write) for the first four draws, and a red line when that shows a nonzero `first` or a count past the device's limit (such a draw is not drawn).
  - A dispatch (or a copy) is not allowed in a render pass, so an indirect mesh draw met with a pass open ends it, and the next draw opens it again with `LOAD`: on a tiler, a store and a load of the attachments per such draw. The log line `indirect mesh draws: N so far, M of them ended an open pass` counts them (at 1, 16, 256, …). Making all of a pass's records before it begins, as `ixc_run` does on Metal ([Indirect draws and dispatches read by the GPU](#indirect-draws-and-dispatches-read-by-the-gpu)), would avoid it.
  - `nvnCommandBufferMultiDrawMeshTasksIndirect`, `MultiDrawMeshTasksIndirectCount` and the 3D forms are still on the stub: their signatures are not confirmed, and no run has shown them called.
  - The Mac host keeps every mesh draw on the stub.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (from the analysis of ocarina-recomp, the same `main` build; not yet run on the phone).

### agl's NDSR upscaler network replaced by FSR 1 EASU on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) agl's scene upscaler network, the one HMMA program of a gameplay frame (Zelda: Ocarina of Time's `fa3033d05c0a41d6`, `agl_ndsr_vr_light.pck`, 32x36 groups of 128), takes about 7.2 ms of a 33 ms GPU frame on the Adreno 830 (`DM_VK_GPUPROF=15`): 22 % of the frame for a 1.33x upscale of 1536x864 to 2048x1152.
- **Cause:** a platform limit. The network is built on Ampere's tensor instruction (HMMA), which the Adreno does not have; the translator lowers it to plain arithmetic ([Ampere HMMA and warp shuffles of agl's upscaler on Adreno](shaders.md#ampere-hmma-and-warp-shuffles-of-agls-upscaler-on-adreno)), which stays far slower than on the Switch 2. The network is purely spatial: one sampled input (the anti-aliased scene, at the scene size), one storage output (at the upscaler's output size), its weights in a read-only storage buffer and its sizes in the push bank (c24); no history, motion or depth. In the light upscaler its input holds sqrt(colour), packed as RGB9E5 by the blend before it (`a335c1db6270c355`, its disassembly; ocarina-recomp's stand-in squares too), and its output the colour.
- **Fix (a workaround, opt-in, with a quality loss):** `DM_VK_NDSR=fsr` (live) runs our own compute in the network's dispatch slot, with its descriptors, layout and group count (`vka_ndsr.c`, one hook in `vka_draw.c` `vka_dispatch`; the network's pipeline is then not made): FSR 1 EASU from the network's input to its output (`dm/gfx/vk_android/fsr/ndsr_easu.comp`; SPIR-V in `vka_ndsr_spv.h`, made by `fsr/ndsr_spv.py`), the sizes from the bound images (any scene size), a grid stride over the game's groups. An output whose format has no storage support (Zelda's `B10G11R11_UFLOAT` on Adreno, which `vka_tex.c` `view_of` leaves unbound as a float storage image, so the network's own stores go nowhere there) gets the module's packed variant: each texel stored as its format's 32-bit word (`B10G11R11_UFLOAT` or `R8G8B8A8_UNORM`) into an `R32_UINT` image, the output binding entered in the dispatch's `vka_imgrt` as an unsigned image so `stage_writes` asks `id_view` for an `R32_UINT` view (the pair learned, the image remade with storage use through it, as for `d17eefaff4f0bc23`'s packed output); another format without storage support is refused (`[RED]`). It takes any entry of the network's interface: a compute entry with the HMMA helper, exactly one combined 2D float sampler, one written 2D float storage image, one read-only storage buffer and the push bank, and nothing else but the support buffer and driver segments (39 agl networks of `agl_ndsr.pck` and `agl_ndsr_vr_light.pck`, none of NDVE's movie programs); the module's placeholder bindings are patched to the entry's. `DM_VK_NDSR_INPUT=sqrt` stores max(c, 0) squared (the light upscaler), `linear` (the default) what EASU gives. `DM_VK_NDSR=bilinear` is an A/B kernel; `DM_VK_NDSR_MAP=push` maps through the push bank (origin c24[1].xy, scale and offset c24[5], read from the network's disassembly, not yet from values: `DM_VK_NDSR_LOG=1` logs the bank). The log says `[vk] NDSR network <key> replaced by FSR 1 EASU (input sqrt, full-image mapping, 1536x864 -> 2048x1152; texture IDs ...; output VkFormat 122 without storage support: packed words through an R32_UINT view)` (or `stored as floats`) once per size, or `[RED] vk: NDSR network <key> not replaced (DM_VK_NDSR): <why>` (its images retyped, its input or output unbound, an output format with no storage support and no packed store). Unset, nothing changes. The cheap anti-aliasing passes before it (0.66 ms) stay. Checked on the PC (2026-11-07): `dm/tools/tests/test_ndsr_standin.py` runs the module on Mesa's lavapipe with its own binding numbers (`selftest/ndsr_lvp.c`: every texel written, the bilinear kernel within R11G11B10F rounding of a CPU reference, EASU's mean within 0.3 % of it where an unsquared one is 32 % off, 1:1, linear input, a 1280x720 scene, the push mapping in pixel and normalized units and with an origin; the packed variant into an `R32_UINT` image within one rounding step of the float store as `B10G11R11` words, and as `RGBA8` words within 2/255 of the reference), and the match rule on synthetic records. Since 2026-11-18 the EASU is a lighter one (`fsr/fsr_easu_light.h`, also the present's `DM_VK_FSR` EASU in `fsr/fsr_easu.frag`), after the ideas in atyuwen's "Optimizing FSR" (written anew from FSR 1): the edge analysis runs once, on the luma bilinearly interpolated at the sample point, instead of at the 4 nearest texels and blended; where that luma has no edge (|dir|^2 < 1/128) the result is the bilinear of the 4 nearest texels, without the 12 Lanczos weights; the colour math is `mediump` (RelaxedPrecision), the coordinates highp; still 12 texel fetches and the deringing clamp, and the sqrt / linear input and the packed store as before. `DM_VK_EASU_FULL=1` (live) brings back AMD's full EASU (`ndsr_easu.comp`'s kernel 3, `fsr_easu.frag`'s constant `FULL`). In TOTKRecompAndroid (the same shader, POCO F7 Ultra, Adreno 830) EASU 1280x717 to 1920x1080 went from 1.00 to 0.50 ms of GPU a frame with edges that looked the same. Here (2026-11-18, POCO F7 Ultra, 891.14, test app, Ocarina of Time at Link's porch, the network's slot 976x549 -> 2048x1152, `DM_VK_GPUPROF` with `DM_VK_EASU_FULL` switched live in one run): 0.66 ms (median of 32 timed frames, 0.64-0.73) against 0.77 ms for AMD's full EASU (28 frames, 0.76-0.85), 0.1 ms a frame; the present's EASU is not in the profiler's frame and was not timed. On lavapipe both pass `selftest/ndsr_lvp.c` (the lighter: EASU's mean 0.6 % under the bilinear reference, AMD's 0.3 %).
- **Open:** the phone (the time against 7.2 ms; the picture against the network's, by an `nxreplay` A/B of a gameplay capture). The input is right only once `a335c1db6270c355`'s store lands (`[RED] vk: shader a335c1db6270c355 binding 3: texture ID ... has no view of shape 0x1 (storage)`): its binding 3 is a float storage image the shader writes raw RGB9E5 words into (bitcast to float), bound to an `E5B9G9R9` texture registered with an `R32_UINT` view; `img_retype_of` keeps it float because `vka_tex_id_int_class` answers the texture's class, not the view's, so it needs the view's integer class (then a uint binding and an `R32_UINT` view of the `E5B9G9R9` image, [the game note](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md)). Until then the network and the stand-in both read the scene target's stale bits as RGB9E5. The push bank decode; the input encoding of Donkey Kong Bananza's and Mario Kart World's `agl_ndsr.pck` networks (unchecked: their profiles have no `ndsr_input`, so the launcher passes `linear`). Launcher (2026-11-08, compile-checked only, not yet run on the phone): the profile key `ndsr_input` (`sqrt` / `linear`, [the key](../../dm/games/README.md#keys)) and a per-game **Upscaler** in the Android launcher's Game settings, offered for every game whose shader map has agl's NDSR networks (`Profile.ndsrEnv`: `DM_VK_NDSR_INPUT` from the key at every start, `DM_VK_NDSR=fsr` when chosen; the user's env lines win). It stays opt-in, not the default. The lighter EASU on the phone (its time against AMD's with `DM_VK_EASU_FULL`, the picture at edges), for the stand-in and the present's `DM_VK_FSR`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (2026-11-07, PC only).

### A temporal upscaler in agl's NDSR slot: camera motion from depth (Android, experimental)

- **Symptom:** (Android) agl's NDSR network is spatial (one input, one output, no history, motion or depth), and its stand-in, FSR 1 EASU (`DM_VK_NDSR=fsr`), is spatial too: edges crawl when the camera moves, and a smaller scene (the 540p and 480p presets) looks soft. A temporal upscaler (SGSR2, FSR 3.1) needs motion vectors and depth, which the game does not make.
- **Cause:** a missing input, not a bug: Ocarina of Time draws no velocity target and uses no jitter. The PC ports reconstructed the camera's motion from the scene depth and the scene's camera block (NX2navi, Lichii #606; `ANALISE-SGSR2-FSR31-FRAMEGEN-2026-11-16.md` (kept local)).
- **Fix (opt-in, default off):** `DM_VK_NDSR=gsr2` (`vka_ndsr.c` "The temporal path"): at the network's dispatch, the camera's motion from depth (`fsr/ndsr_tsr.comp`, our own: each texel's device depth taken to this frame's clip space and through `prev_VP * inverse(VP)` to the previous frame's, the motion in texels, previous minus current, as DLSS takes it; the reprojection computed in double on the CPU, `vka_tsr_cam.h`, as NX2navi learned), the colour squared for agl's sqrt input (`DM_VK_NDSR_INPUT=sqrt`, the profile's `ndsr_input`), the depth written reversed; then GSR2 3-pass (`vka_dlss.c` `vka_dlss_gsr2_views`, the DLSS stand-in's tune and RCAS) from the network's input to its output; the network's dispatch runs a no-op in its slot (`fsr/ndsr_noop.comp`). No jitter. The camera is read from the scene constant block, so only on builds whose layout is known (`k_tsr_cam`, by main's build ID: Ocarina of Time v131072, a 0x3500-byte block, view rows 0-2 and projection rows 7-10, vertex slot 3 first, then any graphics stage's buffer of that size); the depth is the last D32F depth target of the input's size the replay set this frame (`vka_ndsr_note_rt`, a hook at `vka_exec.c` `OP_SET_RT`), else the D32F image of that size used this frame (a scan of the live images, cached by serial). The history starts again on a new size, another depth image, a frame not right after the last, or a camera cut (`vka_tsr_cut`: more than `DM_VK_NDSR_TSR_CUT_T` world units, default 500, or `DM_VK_NDSR_TSR_CUT_A` radians, default 0.6). Without a layout, camera, depth or a blit-capable output it falls back to EASU (one `[RED] vk: NDSR temporal: <why>: FSR 1 EASU this frame` per change of reason). `DM_VK_NDSR_CAM_LOG=<frames>` (any mode) logs the block's rows, the camera position, the projection's jitter terms `P[0][2]` `P[1][2]`, whether the depth is reversed, and the depth image chosen and how. Conventions as switches until checked: `DM_VK_NDSR_TSR_YUP` (default 1: NVN's upper-left origin, NDC y up at the top row), `DM_VK_NDSR_TSR_REV` (-1 from the projection). Checked on the PC: `t_android_tsr_cam.c` (the inverse; the reprojection against both cameras' projections for 40 random walks and turns in each depth mode, reversed or not, and window origin, world coordinates in the thousands, depths from 0.5 to 20000 and the sky: within 0.05 texel for reversed depth; the cut rule), and `selftest/tsr_lvp.c` on Mesa's lavapipe (`test_ndsr_tsr.py`): the GPU's motion equals the CPU reference within the RGBA16F store's rounding in both depth modes and origins, the reversed depth, the colour pass; spirv-val clean.
- **Open:** everything on the phone. The block's layout is the ports' semantics, not checked on this build by disassembly (no disassembler run this session) or by values: diagnostic-only until a `DM_VK_NDSR_CAM_LOG` run shows orthonormal view rows that follow the camera and a projection that does not change with a still camera. The `OP_SET_RT` hook is a one-line patch not yet applied (until then the depth comes from the scan). Characters and wind-moved foliage move by themselves: only GSR2's depth clip and velocity rejection keep them from ghosting (a per-object pass or a bias mask, as NX2navi does, is the next step). The camera block is read when the dispatch is recorded, from the bindings left by the last draw: a post pass that rebinds the block's slot would hide it. Cost not measured (estimate: two render-size passes, 0.1-0.2 ms, plus GSR2's 1.5-1.8 ms against EASU's 0.66 ms at the porch).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (2026-11-20, PC only).

### NVN2's DLSS on a stand-in upscaler (Android)

- **Symptom:** (Android) a game that calls NVN2's DLSS (`nvnCommandBufferEvaluateDlss`) showed its 3D only through a bilinear blit of the render-size colour: Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave's jittered scene shook by a fraction of a pixel every frame and its edges stayed aliased; Star Fox's picture was a plain scale.
- **Cause:** the network has no Vulkan counterpart, and the port of upstream's HLE (`b089126`) kept only upstream's mode 1 (the blit). Upstream Banana Native draws the evaluation with temporal stand-ins of its own from the same inputs the game hands DLSS (colour, reversed depth, render-size motion vectors with their scale, the jitter, the history reset).
- **Fix:** ported from Banana Native (MIT; eden/v0.3.45 `9c98a63b`): `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_dlss.c` (FSR 1 with the jitter taken out, `DM_VK_DLSS=2`; Snapdragon GSR2 3-pass, `3`, the default, with the m2 tune and the five Motion clarity steps `DM_VK_GSR2_CLARITY` 0..4; the encoding copy), `vka_fsr3.c` (FSR 3.1's upscaler passes from the FidelityFX SDK 1.1.4, `4`; FSR 1 where its inputs do not fit, bilinear after that), the shaders in `fsr/dlss_*.comp`, `gsr2/` (BSD-3-Clause, `LICENSE` and `NOTICE.md` there) and `fsr3/*.h` (`dm/third_party/fidelityfx-fsr3`, MIT; `dm/tools/fsr3_shaders.py`), and `vka_exec.c` `dlss_eval` with upstream's fallback order (`vka_dlss.h` `vka_dlss_mode_after`). Adapted: the declarations and the tune table in `vka_dlss.h` (our `vka.h` is not changed); every pass of both files forgets the draws' bound-state memo after its own pipeline and push descriptors (`vka_dyn_invalidate`): `vka_draw.c` `push_same` skips a dispatch's push when it equals the last one, and the stand-in's push on its own layout in between would have left the game's next identical push skipped (upstream binds the same way; our dedupe makes it a hazard); `dlss_eval` ends an open pass first (`vka_rp_end`); `vka_fsr3.c` takes `vkDestroyDescriptorSetLayout` from the device (our table lacks it). The SPIR-V headers are upstream's, rebuilt word for word from their shaders with the NDK's glslc r28 and r30 (`dm/gfx/vk_android/fsr/tsr_spv.py --check`), spirv-val clean (also the eleven FSR 3.1 headers). Host shaders only: `DMSH_VK_CHAIN` stays `45cedbd878f7`. Tests: `dm/host/tests/t_android_dlss.c` (the tune rows, the fallback order, the sRGB plan), `dm/tools/tests/test_ndsr_tsr.py` (the headers). Upstream's measurements (OnePlus Pad 3, Adreno 830, Fire Emblem): GSR2 1.76 ms a frame, FSR 1 1.30 ms, bilinear 0.08 ms, FSR 3.1 about 3 ms on the stock 512.800 (2 ms more than GSR2 on Turnip), with 30-40 % less flicker than GSR2 in pans.
- **Open:** not yet run here (no phone this session). `fsr3_shaders.py --check` was not run: it fetches the SDK from GitHub (or takes `--sdk`), and no checkout is on the drive; the headers came byte for byte from upstream, where all eleven matched with glslang 16.6.0. Upstream's launcher rows (`dlss_standin`, `DlssTune.java`) are not ported: the modes are env switches here.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md#found-on-this-game), [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md#found-on-this-game).

### The DLSS stand-in ignored sRGB textures (Android)

- **Symptom:** (Android) Star Fox's cutscene picture too bright, with `[RED] vk: DLSS stand-in: input and output differ in sRGB (NVN formats 0x38 → 0x29): a blit, whose picture is off in brightness` (2026-11-18, POCO F7 Ultra).
- **Cause:** the replay stores an 8-bit sRGB texture as its UNORM twin and adds the sRGB decode only in its views (`vka_fmt.vk` / `.view`, vka_tex.c). Star Fox hands DLSS an RGBA8 sRGB colour (NVN `0x38`, stored as `R8G8B8A8_UNORM`) and an RGBA16F output (NVN `0x29`). The ported stand-in was a `vkCmdBlitImage` between the two images: a blit converts by the images' formats, so it moved the colour's encoded values into the float output as they were, and the game's post chain read them as linear: every mid-tone lifted (0.5 encoded is 0.21 linear). Upstream found the same on players' reports with its FSR modes ("The DLSS stand-in ignored sRGB textures", eden/v0.3.42).
- **Fix:** general, by the textures' NVN formats (`vka_exec.c` `dlss_eval`, `vka_dlss.h` `vka_dlss_srgb_plan`): the colour is read through the texture's own view format (an sRGB texture decodes) in every mode, an sRGB output is written encoded (RCAS's last pass for FSR 1 and GSR2, the encoding copy `fsr/dlss_copy.comp` for FSR 3.1 and bilinear), and a blit is used only where both textures agree. GSR2, now the default, reads Star Fox's colour decoded into its linear output. Formats that are no sRGB twin are as before (Fire Emblem's RGBA16F to RGBA16F). The first evaluation, and each change, logs `DLSS stand-in: colour NVN format 0x38 (VkFormat 37, sRGB: read decoded), output NVN format 0x29 (VkFormat 97, linear)`. Unit test: `t_android_dlss.c` (Star Fox's pair, Fire Emblem's, a UNORM texture over an image an sRGB texture made, a texture of another format than its image, unknown formats).
- **Open:** the phone check of Star Fox's brightness in its opening cutscene, every mode.
- **Found on:** [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md#found-on-this-game).

### The DLSS network resized

- **Symptom:** (Android) Star Fox: `[RED] vk: DLSS stand-in: rectangles past the textures (input 0,0 768x432 of 768x432, output 0,0 1920x1080 of 768x432): clamped` at every start, after `[nvn] DLSS network resized to 768x432`.
- **Cause:** NVN2 lets a game resize a DLSS network instead of building a new one: `nvnDlssNetworkResize(network, w, h)` sets the network's output resolution, the one size a network has (`nvnDlssNetworkBuilderSetOutputResolution`; the input size comes with each evaluation, `SetInputSubrectangleSize`). The HLE logged it and kept the builder's 1920x1080, so every evaluation asked for a 1920x1080 output rectangle of the 768x432 output texture Star Fox hands it.
- **Fix:** `hle_nvn.c` `nvnDlssNetworkResize` stores the new size in the network (logged for its first 8 changes, `[nvn] DLSS network resized to 768x432 (from 1920x1080): its output size from now on`). Star Fox's evaluation is then 768x432 to 768x432 (DLSS as anti-aliasing at its render size; GSR2's sample factor is 1). The clamp stays for any other mismatch.
- **Found on:** [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md#found-on-this-game).

### Pipelines compiled with the game's floating-point environment on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android, Qualcomm 891.11) a scene far too bright, the same every frame and independent of timing: Ocarina of Time's gameplay (Link's house) was near white-yellow with Link a dark silhouette, already before the tone map (the scene target's median at the bloom pass 2^6 to 2^7 against 2^-6 to 2^-5 in `nxreplay`). A replay of a capture of that frame (`nxreplay`, its own process) rendered it right from the same captured state: the frame's draws, render conditions, sampled textures and uniform buffers matched byte for byte (`DM_VK_TEXSTAT_FS` with `DM_VK_TEXSTAT_AT=1`, hashes of the buffers at the draw), only the colour the main pass's materials wrote differed. Forcing the game to wait for the GPU at each submit (`DM_VK_SUBMIT_WAIT=2`), re-uploading every CPU texture (`DM_VK_RETRACK=2`), the raw memory table (`DM_VK_GMEM_SKIP_LOW`, a diagnostic since removed), CPU / GPU coherence (`DM_VK_COHERE`) and an empty pipeline cache changed nothing.
- **Cause:** the game's code runs natively with the FPCR its SDK sets on its threads (Ocarina of Time: `0x1000000`, flush-to-zero), and the host's code it calls runs on with it: NVN calls branch straight into the host (`nvn_thunks.S`), and a thread created there inherits the creating thread's FP state (Linux copies it at clone). The replay worker (`vk-replay`) logged the game's `0x1000000`, and so the threads made from it and the driver's shader compiler ran with flush-to-zero. Qualcomm's compiler runs in the game's process (Metal's runs in a service of its own), and the pipelines it compiled under flush-to-zero render differently from the same SPIR-V and state compiled with FPCR 0. Which construct it folds or flushes is not known (open). The pipeline cache kept those binaries: with the host's FPCR set but the old cache file read, the scene stayed white; with the fix and no cache, or a new cache, it is right.
- **Fix:** the host's floating-point environment (FPCR 0) on the replay's side (`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_dev.c` `vka_host_fp`): every replay thread (`vk-replay`, `vk-enc`, `vk-complete`, `vk-present`, `vk-psopt`, `vk-prewarm`, `vk-pcache`, `vk-upload`) sets it when it starts, and the calls that compile (device creation in `vka_init`, so the driver's own threads start with it; `vkCreateShaderModule`, `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` and `vkCreateComputePipelines` through the table, `vka_host_compile_fns`) set it around themselves on any thread and restore the caller's. A replay run inline on the game's thread (`DM_VK_WORKER=0`, or no worker thread) sets it around each submission and present (`vka_worker.c`), and the present's own pipelines go through the table too (`vka_present.c` `rk_load`). The pipeline cache's file names carry a generation (`VKA_PCACHE_GEN` in `vka.h`, `-fp0`): `vk_pipelines-fp0.bin` with the system driver, `vk_pipelines-<driver>-fp0.bin` with a custom one; the old default files (`vk_pipelines.bin`, `vk_pipelines-<driver>.bin`), compiled the old way, are removed at start (`vka_pso.c`), so the first run after the change compiles again (the prewarm list stays). `nxreplay`'s own cache beside it is `vk_pipelines-fp0.bin` (its old `vk_pipelines.bin`, which may have been seeded from a live file compiled under flush-to-zero, is removed), seeded only from a live file of that generation; the capture header's `vk_pcache` names the file the live run used (it named `$DM_TEMP/vk_pipelines.bin`, wrong with a custom driver, and since this change a file that no longer exists). `DM_VK_GUEST_FPCR=1` (restart) brings the old behaviour back for a comparison; the cache then keeps no file, so no later run reads pipelines compiled that way. Checked on the phone (2026-11-07, Qualcomm 891.11): Link's house lit correctly, 23.8 fps on a hot phone (45 C). Open: a file named by `DM_VK_PCACHE=<file>` is used as named, so one written before the change still holds pipelines compiled the old way (delete it). The rest of the host (the HLE and SVC handlers through `entry.S`, which saves the game's FPCR but does not set the host's, the NVN thunks, the audio and movie threads) also runs with the game's FPCR; nothing there is known to depend on it (a general fix would set FPCR 0 at each game-to-host boundary, an `msr fpcr` a call). A driver thread made lazily from a call on a game thread would inherit the game's FPCR; none is known to compile there. Not yet run on the phone: the inline replay, and Mario Kart World and Donkey Kong Bananza (whether their threads run with flush-to-zero too: the `started with the game's FPCR` log line; a cold pipeline cache once).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (2026-11-07, POCO F7 Ultra, Qualcomm 891.11).

### Android frame replay loses GPU placement of raw-pointer pools

- **Symptom:** a frame replay hangs inside a task program or silently loses mesh geometry, although its pool bytes were captured and all regions mapped at their original addresses. A bounded diagnostic loop records repeated raw-address-map misses; shader data reads return zero. Skipping unsupported mesh draws had hidden the missing placement.
- **Cause:** `CAP_PADD` preserved the list of CPU-visible NVN pools, but not which ranges were GPU-addressable in the live process. Recreating those pools lazily and placing only explicit command operands misses memory reached through a pointer inside another buffer. The live address-taking APIs already request placement; it is the replay that dropped that state. CPU address preservation alone is insufficient.
- **Fix:** Android captures now write `CAP_PLAC` chunks independently of `CAP_PADD`. `vka_placed_ranges` snapshots actual GPU-addressable segments; capture clips alignment padding to captured pool storage. Replay restores those ranges before the corresponding submission and verifies complete GPU coverage, preserving lazy allocation for unrelated CPU-visible texture pools. Placement changes also trigger the replay's inter-submission state wait. Legacy captures remain readable but warn that this metadata is absent. `DM_VK_REPLAY_RAW_NEED` accepts explicit comma-separated addresses only as a legacy replay diagnostic; no game profile or live rendering path contains those addresses.
- **Validation:** the Android capture-format test passes 86 checks, including preservation and relocation of a partial GPU range without expanding it to every CPU pool. For the legacy Zelda capture, a task-prefix bisection and raw-read probe identified an omitted pool; restoring it made the unmodified task finish and emit geometry without any loop cap. A later mesh probe identified another omitted pool. The production-library replay then completed all task/mesh draws. Exact per-game results and remaining live acceptance are in the door investigation (`ANALISE-ZELDA-DOOR-2026-11-10.md`, kept local). A large permitted image mismatch is still not a visual-comparison pass.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game).

### Turnip hangs in the first 3D frame of a cold run

- **Symptom:** (Android, Turnip) the device is lost in the first heavy 3D frame of a run without a pipeline cache: `[RED] vk: upload batch N: the wait failed after 0.00 s (-4: the device is lost)`, `part of a submission: -4`, then `GPU hang: vkWaitSemaphores -4 (the device is lost)` and the host exits. Before 2026-11-08 the first line read `upload batch N not done in 60.0 s (-4)`, which looked like the replay's own timeout firing behind slow pipeline compiles: it printed the hang time, not the wait's, and -4 is `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST`, returned at once. Runs with a pipeline cache of the library went on.
- **Cause:** a real GPU hang, not the replay's timeout and not the upload thread starved behind compiles: Mesa's kgsl backend reports the context guilty (`kgsl_device_check_status`: "GPU faulted or hung"); KGSL makes a context guilty for a long-command timeout (`GMU_GPU_SW_HANG`) or a burst of faults, not for one page fault. The loss is only seen at the replay's next wait, often tens of seconds later, because the parts of the submission being recorded signal nothing the completion thread waits for. Ocarina of Time's first 3D frame after a cold start is one submission of about 13,300 draws (recorded over 30-50 s of pipeline compiles) with depth-only passes over a 4096x4096 D32 target, a 12288x12288 D16 target (serial 6390) and a 4096x4096 D16 target (serial 6391). `DM_VK_PARTLOG=1` with `DM_VK_ZLOG=1` put the first part never seen finished at the one that ends the 12288x12288 pass (begun with LOAD after the part splits before it) and begins the pass over 6391 with a depth clear (loadOp CLEAR); every draw there tests and writes depth with LESS and depth bias on, with no compare direction change, no write with ALWAYS / NOT_EQUAL, no stencil, no discard and no clear, game barrier or timestamp inside the pass, so nothing in the draws turns Turnip's LRZ off or on. What is particular is 6391's first content: the image got its memory in that frame (a zero-shared image's own memory at its first write; with `DM_VK_ZSHARE=0` the same image's first use) and its zero clear (`vkCmdClearDepthStencilImage`, which on Turnip also clears the image's LRZ buffer and its fast-clear / direction state) was recorded into the upload batch, another command buffer that goes to the queue before the part (`vka_dev.c` "Upload batches").
