The device was not lost when that clear was recorded into the part itself (`DM_VK_UPLOAD_BATCH=0`), nor with a bottom-of-pipe timestamp, which Turnip records as a wait for idle, after the barrier that ends a pass (`DM_VK_XP_TS=1`) or before a depth pass begins (`DM_VK_XP_TS=4`, also when narrowed to a new depth image, to a pass with a depth clear or to the pass after one of 8192 pixels or more, `DM_VK_XP_TS_SEL`), nor with LRZ off (`TU_DEBUG=nolrz`) or `DM_VK_GPUPROF=1` (a timestamp after every full barrier). It still was with the game's barriers ending the pass (`DM_VK_BAR_KEEP=0`), without the command encoder thread (`DM_VK_ENC=0`), without zero-shared images (`DM_VK_ZSHARE=0`), with a wait for idle at the upload batch's end (`DM_VK_XP_TS=2`), in sysmem mode (`TU_DEBUG=sysmem`), without concurrent binning (`TU_DEBUG=nocb`) and with `DM_VK_GPL=0`. No valid-usage rule of the replay's was found broken: every image is in `GENERAL`, the batch runs before the part in queue order and ends in a full barrier, and a full barrier (`ALL_COMMANDS`, memory write to read and write) separates the passes. So the hang is in Turnip's LRZ handling (Adreno 840, Mainline Turnip v32): a depth pass that clears an image whose LRZ was first set up in an earlier command buffer, begun right after a pass over another depth target with LRZ on, without the GPU idle between them; which command the GPU waits on is not known (open, for a reduced case for Mesa).
- **Fix (a workaround for the driver):** a depth / stencil image's first content (its zero clear at its first use, `img_first_use`, and the clear of the memory of its own a zero-shared image gets, `img_own`) is recorded into the submission being recorded whenever no pass is open there, instead of the upload batch (`vka_tex.c` `ds_here`; `DM_VK_DS_FIRST_HERE=0`, live, as before). It holds for every driver: a clear has no staging for the batch to retire, and uploads stay in the batch. Doing this only at the pass's begin was not enough (the device was still lost in 3 of 3 cold runs): Ocarina of Time's 6391 is first touched by the game's depth clear (`clear_ds`), which makes the image before its pass. Diagnostics added: `DM_VK_PARTLOG` gives each part's depth state (compare ops, direction flips, writes with ALWAYS / NOT_EQUAL, stencil, bias, clears, kept barriers and timestamps inside a pass), `DM_VK_ZLOG` each draw of the large depth passes, `DM_VK_XP_TS` / `DM_VK_XP_TS_SEL` the timestamp experiment; the GPU hang lines list every submission not yet done with how long before the hang it went to the queue, and how many parts of the submission being recorded went to the queue and were seen finished; parts that ran 200 ms or more are logged as they finish (`GPU time: part …`), every part and every split inside a pass with `DM_VK_PARTLOG=1`.
- **Open:** the driver-side mechanism, and a reduced case for Mesa (two depth-only passes over different D16 targets in one command buffer, the second's image cleared with `vkCmdClearDepthStencilImage` in an earlier submission and begun with loadOp CLEAR).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on the Honor PNM-N49 (Adreno 840, Mainline Turnip v32), every cold run at frames 1,884-1,932 (libraries v-c3a177c4698f and v-72c5210a46e5: 6 of 6 and 9 of 9 cold runs with LRZ on, the clear through the upload batch and no wait for idle between the passes). With the fix, 2 of 2 cold runs on v-72c5210a46e5 with LRZ on finished the frame (every part read) and went on to frames 1,996-2,023 at 110 s; `DM_VK_DS_FIRST_HERE=0` lost the device again (1 of 1); a warm Turnip run reached frame 2,827 and the system driver 4,832, no loss.

### Frame replays that diverged from the live frame on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android) `nxreplay` of a capture draws another picture than the live frame: Ocarina of Time's Kokiri Forest frame was covered in white fog blobs, 2.3 M of its 2.36 M scene pixels differed, and the comparison of drivers per pass that the replayer exists for was impossible. Replay 1 differed from the later ones (Donkey Kong Bananza: replay 1 mostly black, replays 2 and later close; hundreds of MB of pool memory written only in the replay).
- **Cause:** the capture left out state the live frame depended on, and its own steps changed the frame it captured. Found with the per-step comparisons below, in this order:
  1. *Texture pool slots.* With the pool reads reported (`vka_tex.c` "Texture pool reads", `DM_VK_POOL_FB`), a registered texture's slot names its image only once that image exists; a texture read only through the pool gets the null view (reads 0) until a read of it is reported, one submission later. The live frame's slots name what earlier frames asked for; the replayer registered the textures and started with null views in every such slot.
  2. *Parked GPU waits.* Ocarina of Time submits its ambient lighting's consumers on three queues (5, 6, 8) that wait for an event before the producer on queue 1 that signals it, every frame; the replay worker parks them until the producer is replayed ([Android queue dependencies](nvn-objects.md#android-queue-dependencies-and-texture-object-ownership), `vka_qsched.h`). The capture waited for the GPU at each submit with `mtl_finish`, a CPU wait whose safety valve releases parks: the consumers ran at the next submit, before their producer and after the game's CPU writes in between, so the captured live frame was not the frame the game draws. The capture did not record each submission's queue, so the replayer ran every submission on one queue in order. The worker also releases a park whenever it looks (each job, every millisecond, a 200 ms park time), points no capture step marks, and the event word the consumers wait for is written by the CPU after the frame's last submit, which no step captured.
  3. *Render conditions.* A condition on a report still in flight is unknown and draws ([Render conditions on occlusion reports and reports in flight](#render-conditions-on-occlusion-reports-and-reports-in-flight-on-the-vulkan-replay)): about 660 decisions a frame depend on whether the GPU had finished an occlusion report when the condition was recorded, which no replay reproduces (a light probe's face drew other geometry).
  4. *Images that follow their storage.* The capture read back only images the GPU wrote and made the others again from the pool bytes at replay, assuming a live image equals what its storage gives. It does not always: the 3D lookup tables Ocarina of Time's sky and fog read (256x32x16 RGBA16F) held other content live than their storage gives, and the fog computed from them was the white blobs.
  5. *The capture clock.* `DM_NVN_CAPTURE=@<seconds>` counted from the creation of the process, which on Android can be minutes before the game (the `:game` process was 7 minutes old 8 s after Play): `@130` caught a loading frame.
- **Fix:** (1) each registry entry of the capture says whether its slot named its image (`cap_treg` pad bit 1, `vka_cap_slot_named`); the replayer asks for those images the way a reported read does (`vka_cap_slot_want`). (2) The capture's step at each submit waits only for what the GPU was handed, a park left parked (`mtl_finish_handed`, `vka_worker.c`), records each submission's queue (`CAP_SUBQ`), takes the CPU's `nvnEventSignal` calls in the frame as steps of their own (`CAP_CSIG`) and a last step before the present for what the CPU wrote after the last submit; while a capture or a replay runs, a park waits however long the steps take and the parked queues are looked at only once per step, after its state and before its submission (`vka_worker_park_hold`, `vka_worker_poll_now`), in the capture and the replayer alike. The replayer keeps each submission's command copies until its last wait (a parked one is recorded later). (3) The capture records each render-condition decision in recording order with its report address (`CAP_RCND`); the replayer gives the same decision at the same place and address, and decides by itself from the first one that does not match (said in red). (4) The capture reads back every image the frame reaches (about 0.7 GB more for that frame, 2,138 images); `DM_NVN_CAPTURE_READ=storage` leaves storage-following ones to their pool bytes, as before. (5) The clock counts from the host's own start (`g_host_t0_ns`, `dm/host/main.c`). Diagnostics added: `DM_NVN_CAPTURE_IMAGES=1` writes every GPU-written image at the present and a hash of each before every later step, and the replayer names the images that stop matching and the step where each first does (`--dump-live` for their pictures); before each step the replayer compares its pool blocks with the live run's changes (`written by the replay's submit N, other bytes than live` against `written only live`); `DM_RP_PC_DUMP`, `DM_RP_SER` print chosen blocks and serials ([android/TESTING.md](../../android/TESTING.md#frame-capture-and-replay-on-the-phone)).
- **Validation:** Ocarina of Time, Kokiri Forest, frame 4500, POCO F7 Ultra with Qualcomm 891.14 (2026-11-20/21, ten captures). Before: 2,317,000 of 3,686,400 presented pixels differ, the fog white. After each fix the first step where images differ moved later (submit 3 with 19 images, then only after the last submit, then only the probe face, then the lookup tables' outputs); with all five, three replays of one capture differ from the live frame in 132 to 197 pixels (largest byte difference 11 to 13): a match with `--max-pixels 200`. All 685 render-condition decisions were replayed as live, the scheduler parked and released as live (`released early by nothing`), and every hashed image matched before every step.
- **Open:** why a live image that follows its storage holds other content than the storage (the lookup tables above): a live-side question for `vka_tex.c`'s tracking, not answered here. The probe cube's mip levels differ between replays of one capture (5 to 40 of its 48 subresources), so something in their making is not ordered. Replay 1 still ends about 500 more render passes than later replays (first uses).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game).

### The texture serial map grew with every serial ever registered on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android, by code reading; not measured on a phone) in a long session with much texture churn the texture cache's serial map keeps growing (about 32 MB after a million serials), and so does the replay thread's cost to let an image go: `map_del_im` and `img_detach_locked_at` scan the whole table for each image.
- **Cause:** `vka_tex.c`'s serial → image map and its set of serials the CPU wrote before their image existed (cpuw) were open addressing with tombstones whose entry count only went up (a put into a tombstone counted, a delete never took one off), so the table doubled with the serials ever registered rather than the live ones. The map's put also stopped at the first tombstone of a chain without looking further, which would store a live key twice; here `map_put` only runs after a `map_get` miss under `g_tmu` (`vka_tex_get`), so that path was not reached and no wrong image came of it (upstream's entry says otherwise for its tree).
- **Fix:** (2026-11-13, a port of upstream Banana Native v0.3.13, `624c267e`; unit-tested, not phone-checked) `vka_smap.h`: a probe runs past tombstones to the key or an empty slot; a new key goes into the first tombstone seen only after the chain proved it absent; live entries and tombstones are counted apart; the table is made again at its size when tombstones fill it and grows only when live entries need it; without memory a put that cannot be taken leaves the texture without an entry (its next lookup makes the image again). `map_get`, `map_put`, `map_del` and `map_del_im` stay as thin wrappers (`t_android_texlife.c` calls `map_put`), `img_detach_locked_at` counts an image's owners with `vksm_count_im`, and `vka_cap_cpuw` and `cpuw_take` use the same table. No switch: the lookups answer the same, only the table's bookkeeping changed.
- **Check:** `t_android_smap` (`dm/host/tests`: a chain with tombstones before a live key, 400,000 random puts and deletes against a reference with no key stored twice, an image's entries counted and going together, a million short-lived serials leaving the table at its first size). The memory and CPU over a long session on the phone are open.
- **Found on:** upstream Banana Native v0.3.13 (code reading); here every game on the Vulkan replay, none measured yet (open).

### Repeated inline updates of one range kept one GPU-write entry each on the Vulkan replay

- **Symptom:** (Android, by code reading; not measured on a phone) a submission with many inline uniform updates to the same few ranges grows the texture cache's list of GPU writes (`g_gw`) by one entry and one `malloc` per update, and every texture upload from guest storage (`gw_content`), uniform shadow (`gw_shadow_read`) and `vka_tex_prepare` walks the longer list. Upstream counted 11,010 updates to 7 ranges in a Splatoon Raiders submission (its occlusion queries).
- **Cause:** `gw_add` (`vka_tex.c`) merged only `GW_OPAQUE` entries of the same range in a submission. This replay records every inline update there, kept in a pass or not (`vka_exec.c` `OP_UPDATE_UB`; [Inline uniform updates on the Vulkan replay](#inline-uniform-updates-on-the-vulkan-replay)).
- **Fix:** (2026-11-13, a port of upstream Banana Native v0.3.12, `03f5e80b`, PR #55; not phone-checked) a `GW_UPDATE` of exactly the range of one of the last 16 entries not yet fenced copies its bytes into that entry, which moves to the newest place with a new seq. The walk back stops at a `GW_COPY` (it may have read the old bytes) and at any other write overlapping the range, so nothing between the old place and the end touched those bytes and the content at the end is the same; an image uploaded in between has a `gw_seen` below the new seq and is uploaded again once the fence is done, as for a new entry. `DM_VK_GW_MERGE=0` (live) keeps one entry per update, as before.
- **Found on:** upstream Banana Native v0.3.12 (Splatoon Raiders); here no game measured yet (open: the list's length and `vka_tex_prepare`'s time on the phone).

### The DLSS stand-in wrote alpha 1 over the colour's alpha (Android)

- **Symptom:** (Android, NVN2's DLSS on the GSR 2 or FSR 1 stand-in) a screen that draws its 3D layer over a 2D one shows only the 3D layer on black: Fast Fusion's main menu showed a lit ship and no menu, which still worked blind; the Bilinear upscaler showed it. No `[RED]` line.
- **Cause:** the game composites the DLSS output over its menu with SRC_ALPHA / ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA blending (`/shaders/fullscreen.shdr`, `b24925420d945897`), so it needs the colour's alpha in the output (0 around the ship, 1 on it), as NVN2's DLSS keeps it. The stand-in's last pass, RCAS (`fsr/dlss_rcas.comp`), stored alpha 1 everywhere (EASU and GSR 2 keep colour only), and the blit carried it into the output. `DM_VK_TEX_STATS=b24925420d945897` with `DM_VK_TEX_STATS_DRAWS=12` showed the output (texture ID 505) with alpha above 1/1024 on 100 % of its texels on GSR 2 and on 14 % (the ship) with the bilinear blit.
- **Fix:** general: RCAS takes the alpha from the input colour, read bilinearly where the output pixel lies in the input rectangle, jitter included as for the colour (`vka_dlss.c` `vka_dlss_run`, the colour view on binding 1). FSR 3.1 (`DM_VK_DLSS=4`, `vka_fsr3.c`) now preserves it the same way through `vka_dlss_scale_alpha` (`fsr/dlss_copy.comp`).
- **Found on:** Fast Fusion (`040041E0212AE000`, no profile here; [its note in Banana Native](https://git.eden-emu.dev/moceure/BananaNative/src/tag/eden/v0.3.50/dm/games/040041E0212AE000/README.md)) (main menu, 2026-11-19, Odin 3 on the system driver and Turnip v32).

### DLSS debug overlay setters

- **Symptom:** `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnDlssParametersSetOverlayDebugLayer`, `nvnDlssParametersSetOverlayFullScreen`, `nvnDlssParametersSetOverlayJitterDebug`, or `nvnDlssParametersSetOverlayShowNans` at startup or when configuring DLSS.
- **Cause:** NVN2 exposes debug overlay setters on `NVNdlssParameters` that games call during initialization (usually with 0).
- **Fix:** implement the four setters as no-ops in `hle_nvn.c`.
- **Found on:** Fast Fusion (`040041E0212AE000`, no profile here; [its note in Banana Native](https://git.eden-emu.dev/moceure/BananaNative/src/tag/eden/v0.3.50/dm/games/040041E0212AE000/README.md)) on the Android app on the Odin 3 (system driver and Turnip v32).

### NVN2 draws with 32 vertex attributes (Android)

- **Symptom:** `[RED] BindVertexAttribState n=32` (logged 11,786 times in 50 s) and a black frame on Android.
- **Cause:** NVN2 (Ampere) supports up to 32 vertex attributes, whereas the host kept a 16-attribute cap and dropped the entire `BindVertexAttribState` call when `n > 16`.
- **Fix:** `p_bind_vattr` (`nvn_obj.h`), `vka_dstate` (`vka.h`), and `vka_draw.c` record and replay up to 32 vertex attributes on Android Vulkan; Metal continues to clamp to 16.
- **Found on:** Fast Fusion (`040041E0212AE000`, no profile here; [its note in Banana Native](https://git.eden-emu.dev/moceure/BananaNative/src/tag/eden/v0.3.50/dm/games/040041E0212AE000/README.md)) on the Android app on the Odin 3 (system driver and Turnip v32). Here on [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (17 attributes, `n=17` about 270 times a second): Mr. E's dialog over a black screen (POCO F7 Ultra, 2026-11-22).

### Non-indexed indirect quad draws skipped on the Vulkan replay (Android)

- **Symptom:** `[RED] vk: an indirect draw of quads or a line loop is not on Vulkan yet: skipped` on Android, and geometry drawn with non-indexed `nvnCommandBufferDrawArraysIndirect` (`NVN_DRAW_PRIMITIVE_QUADS`) missing. What stays skipped now says which: `an indirect draw of indexed quads` (or `multi-draw quads`, `a line loop`) `is not on Vulkan yet: skipped`.
- **Cause:** Vulkan has no quad topology, so a direct `QUADS` draw binds indices made on the CPU (`0, 1, 2, 0, 2, 3` a quad, `count / 4 * 6` of them); an indirect draw keeps its vertex count and first vertex in GPU memory, which the CPU cannot read when it records the draw, so `vka_draw.c` skipped every indirect quad or line loop draw.
- **Fix:** the record is converted on the GPU before the pass (`vka_draw.c` "Indirect quad draws"): its 16 bytes are copied into the submission's scratch, the compute shader in `vka_quad_indirect_spv.h` writes `{min(vertexCount / 4, 262144) * 6, instanceCount, 0, firstVertex, firstInstance}` beside them, a `VkDrawIndexedIndirectCommand` (full barrier, then transfer to compute, compute to indirect reads), and the draw is `vkCmdDrawIndexedIndirect` over a shared index buffer of 262,144 quads (6 MiB, `vka_qdx_fill_indices`, made at the first such draw); `vertexOffset = firstVertex` gives the vertex indices of the non-indexed draw. `vka_exec.c`'s look-ahead for indirect mesh draws (`mdx_scan`) converts the records up to the next write before the pass opens (`vka_qdx_batch`), once the run has had such a draw; one it did not reach is converted at its draw, ending an open pass. Indexed quads, multi-draw quads and line loops through indirect records stay skipped, each with its own red line (open). `DM_VK_QUAD_IND=0` (live) skips them as before. Ported from Banana Native v0.3.51, 3ce1aadf (its separate look-ahead joined to this tree's mesh one).
- **Check:** `t_android_quad_ind` (`dm/host/tests`: the SPIR-V's header and execution mode, spirv-val where it is on the PATH, the index pattern and the host reference of the conversion).
- **Found on:** [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Imprisonment](../../dm/games/04003F40232DE000/README.md#found-on-this-game) in Banana Native v0.3.51 (Koei Tecmo's KTGL engine; such draws seen there on *Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity*, `01002B00111A2000`, after boot). Not yet run here.
